## 🎯 Session Goal

Checkpoint 013 established the corrected candidate through an actual bounded normal-service proof, including three publications, collision refusal, live cancellation and fresh-instance recovery. That was still outside Electron. The next intended step was one finite native Journey A through the original shell, genuine folder pickers, initial inspection-only draft, per-file range editor, target execution, output authority and normal closure, with exact source/runtime/security gates intact.

The first native attempt advanced secure startup and normal closure but did not register an input folder. The interaction involved typed-path/autocomplete uncertainty and a stale window handle. Those observations did not establish an application defect or a portal failure. The session therefore evolved from completing the finite native journey to preserving the incomplete attempt, auditing the saved evidence and approving a specifically corrected operator procedure for one further attempt. A2 then exposed a concrete automation text-input limitation; a separately reviewed A3 completed the finite flow. That successful run revealed a genuine minor platform-wording defect, which justified a later isolated, reviewed two-file repair. None of these stages permitted automatic retries or security changes in response to the ambiguous first picker.

The session aimed to:

- Finish checkpoint 013 preservation and bind the native work to its exact 530 tracked files, verified pinned Electron, restored candidate pair, listed dependencies and original raw calibration.
- Use the existing original UI/preload and security posture, not a replacement interface, hidden IPC, renderer injection, alternate runtime or baseline fallback.
- Distinguish actual native startup, folder registration, draft/range readiness, target publication and closure rather than collapse them into “app tested.”
- Preserve a failed/incomplete navigation attempt with its actual diagnostics and cause uncertainty; make any further attempt a new bounded reviewed scope.
- Keep useful read-only next-slice planning separate from implemented/tested source and preserve the full owner-format history.

The intended acceptance boundary stayed narrow: a developer-only native range journey, not generic Full/resize/AV1/HDR/GPU/Windows support, native cancellation parity or release acceptance.

### 10. Fix only the platform wording actually observed

A3 exposed a real minor UI defect: successful Linux/Thunar reveal feedback said “Show-in-folder request sent to Windows.” Unlike the uncertain A1 picker cause, this is an observed text/platform mismatch. The isolated worker first retained an expected failing test, then replaced one renderer feedback string with “Show-in-folder request sent to your file manager.” The existing completed-output expectation was updated and a regression added for Linux-style and Windows-style displayed paths.

The reviewed patch is 2,273 bytes, SHA-256 `f77b34f19b818d53678dfa3a72e9778438051db69e3a513f0350586d79564628`, confined to `ui/renderer.js` and `tests/ui-workbench.test.cjs`. The token-only `{outputId, action}` request, pending-click suppression, current-row/request/token checks, stale-result handling and unverified-playback warning stay unchanged. No main, preload, IPC, output-identity policy, theme, runtime pin or default change is included.

The worker's combined set passed 87/87; independent renderer review passed 67/67 and reported no scoped blocker. After the source freeze release, the lead integrated the exact patch and reran `node --test tests/ui-workbench.test.cjs linux-blue/candidate-workbench-renderer.test.cjs tests/output-access.test.cjs` through the evidence wrapper. `20261117T183737Z_native-reveal-wording-lead` records 87/87, no skips, exit 0, 1,078.768 ms and unchanged covered bytes. ## 🧠 Key Insights

### Secure native startup is useful evidence without being a complete journey

A1 progressed from a version-only executable preflight to the actual original workbench with candidate mode, original UI/preload and intended security flags. Its normal closure also balanced every owned child. That is a meaningful native prerequisite result. The absence of input registration still prevents a claim about draft, range, target encoding or output actions. A precise partial result is more useful than either calling the whole app passed or dismissing all startup evidence because the next interaction stalled.

### An ambiguous picker result is not an established source defect

The route inspection shows how cancelled selection can leave the UI unchanged, but saved artifacts do not reveal the native dialog return value. Operator focus/autocomplete uncertainty and a stale handle are enough to justify a different observed interaction procedure; they are not enough to blame app code or a portal diagnostic. The chosen response preserves the first attempt and creates one specifically bounded follow-up instead of modifying code on speculation.

### Persisted state and clock domains are part of the evidence boundary

A2 deliberately reuses the known fixed state from A1 without clearing it. That preserves history but means it is not a clean-state startup test. Likewise, native diagnostic epochs and outer UTC labels cannot be fused just because the wallclock offset resembles a timezone. Explicit state/clock provenance prevents a neat narrative from claiming a stronger experiment than the one actually performed.

### A reviewed next-slice design is a direction, not implementation authority

The proposed resize family can reuse existing builders and resource machinery, but their existence does not qualify new geometry or resurrect missing old reports. Prospective red tests, private branding and fresh source-bound media gates remain work to do. Read-only planning advanced understanding while the live native source stayed frozen. Later isolated implementation/tests are real but remain outside the authoritative checkout and have no resized-media qualification.

## 📌 Decisions Made

- Confirmed — The engineering lead gated native execution on preserved checkpoint 013, exact 530-file source and verified desktop/runtime/raw-report identities, with original UI/preload/security posture retained.
- Confirmed — A1's result is secure original-workbench startup and normal closure, blocked before input registration. The lead/reviewer retained zero Prepare/Start/target publications without pretending startup probes were zero media children.
- Confirmed — The independent auditor left picker causality unknown. No source patch, portal fix, security bypass or warning suppression was adopted from an ambiguous result.
- Confirmed — The lead authorized a new A2 scope only after A1 closure/audit and independent plan review, with corrected navigation, one picker per role, known persisted state, conditional one finite range encode and no automatic retry/Journey B.
- Confirmed — After the separate harmless key-capability check and independent A3 review, the lead authorized one final bounded native launch. A3 completed the original finite flow; no fourth launch, player or Journey B followed. After final audit/source reconciliation, the lead integrated only the observed platform-neutral wording repair and its tests.
- Confirmed direction / deferred integration — The lead chose the separately reviewed, isolated source-tested E3 proposal as next after checkpoint 014 preservation. Its 125/125 focused and six-test orientation results are not authoritative integration or resize-media evidence; source ownership, affected checks and fresh qualification gates remain required.
- Deferred — Same-service recovery, native cancellation Journey B, broader operation/geometry/platform acceptance, production defaults/catalog, packaging and owner release acceptance remain outside the approved finite work.

## ⚠️ Difficulties / Friction

The first input picker did not produce a confirmed selected directory. Typing/autocomplete and a disappearing dialog made the operator observation uncertain, and stale-handle input returned `BadWindow`. The absence of a retained native return value or standalone screenshot files prevents an independent cause assignment. That uncertainty cost the intended range journey, but it did not justify changing code or weakening the native security posture.

Native logs retained missing D-Bus, SSL handshake and portal atom-cache diagnostics. They should neither be silently suppressed nor treated as proof of the picker cause. The prior x265/topology/native-performance uncertainty also remains separate from successful startup.

The two clock domains differ by approximately two hours. Using raw diagnostic epochs as if they were synchronized with outer UTC would create inaccurate timings. Keeping both record types explicit is less convenient but preserves the actual evidence.

A new process does not mean pristine state: the first native session created known fixed state, and A2 is authorized to reuse it without clearing. Old and new service events must be separated by session identity instead of counting every copied event as part of the newest attempt. Profile/token caches remain outside evidence inspection/sharing.

A2 narrowed the failure to the available text-input capability: the AT-SPI provider could not service the field, and no path was sent. The terminal fallback had executed before the later Sky-only correction arrived, so the record cannot claim Sky-only throughout A2. A3 then required a separate keyboard-only gate rather than quietly extending A2. The successful journey also revealed a genuine wording mismatch and an audit interpretation error around post-cleanup ctime; both were corrected narrowly, without changing media evidence or pretending the old picker cause was solved.

## ❌ Mistakes / Risks Noted

### Ambiguous picker interaction and stale-window input in A1

- What happened or could happen: Actual typed-path/autocomplete uncertainty left the main UI without an input selection; subsequent stale-dialog input returned X11 BadWindow.
- Actual impact: A1 stopped before Prepare, range editing or target execution; no target output was published.
- Potential impact: More unbounded clicking or hidden path injection could exceed the intended attempt or grant the wrong directory; blaming source without evidence could create an unnecessary patch.
- Correction / current status: The first result is preserved. A2 changes navigation under a new explicit bounded approval, not app source or security. A2 later stopped on a confirmed automation text-input capability block; A3 succeeded under a separate global-key gate. Neither later fact establishes A1's exact native return or cause.
- Failure Intelligence: Retain the operator/evidence failure and cause uncertainty in this session and native execution records; no confirmed application-defect incident is asserted.

### Diagnostic messages could be mistaken for a proven picker cause

- What happened or could happen: Actual logs include missing D-Bus, SSL handshake -202 and portal atom-cache messages; no native canceled/filePaths return is retained.
- Actual impact: These warnings/errors are observable but their role in the no-selection outcome is unproven.
- Potential impact: Treating correlation as cause could prompt a security bypass, portal/system change or unjustified “fixed” claim.
- Correction / current status: Read-only route inspection preserves canceled-selection as a compatible but unproven explanation. No workaround or causal clearance was applied.
- Failure Intelligence: Keep the exact logs and audit limits; no unsupported root-cause ticket or duplicate warning incident is needed.

### Clock and screenshot evidence can be overstated

- What happened or could happen: Actual native/outer clock discrepancy and CUA-only A1 screenshots limit independent chronology/visual review.
- Actual impact: A1 has no standalone portable screenshot archive, and cross-domain times cannot be treated as synchronized.
- Potential impact: Invented conversions or claimed screenshot preservation would overstate independently recoverable evidence.
- Correction / current status: Both clock domains and screenshot destinations are explicitly labelled. No clock setting or screenshot-history reconstruction was attempted.
- Failure Intelligence: A documentation/evidence boundary is retained here; no new source defect is alleged.

### Known state reuse is not pristine testing and must not expose profile secrets

- What happened or could happen: A2 reuses fixed persisted state created by the closed first attempt, and copied service diagnostics may contain prior-session events.
- Actual impact: A2 cannot claim an empty-profile experiment. Old/new session evidence must remain distinct.
- Potential impact: Counting old events as new could inflate coverage; copying profile/cache/token databases could expose irrelevant private state.
- Correction / current status: No clearing/editing or profile-store inspection/sharing is authorized. Only scoped service diagnostics and verified identities are retained for the proof.
- Failure Intelligence: This is an explicit controlled setup condition, not an observed data-loss or secret-disclosure incident.

### Native execution does not inherit headless harness hard gates

- What happened or could happen: The native application lacks the service harness's external 180-child admission hook; disk/abort controls remain prospective rather than hard active-write/exit guarantees.
- Actual impact: A1 closed all 28 children with no live remainder; A2 also closed 28/28 and A3 closed 42/42, all with no live remainder and exit 0; those are observed outcomes, not universal caps.
- Potential impact: A future native attempt could have different child/drainage behavior or exceed an admission-time estimate.
- Correction / current status: Each native attempt requires its own bounded review, exact source/identity checks and observed closure; no hard resource guarantee is carried over by analogy.
- Failure Intelligence: Known operational limitation, not an observed runaway or storage-exhaustion incident.

### A read-only design or causal triage could be relabelled as fixed/tested source

- What happened or could happen: The original E3 note and failure review propose/explain work; later E3 implementation/tests occur only in an isolated proposal, while the authoritative patch here is the separately reviewed wording fix.
- Actual impact: The causal triage reran no suite. Isolated E3 tests passed but establish no authoritative integration or geometry/media qualification; the wording patch has its own distinct test evidence.
- Potential impact: Reopening historical factories by map edits or declaring inherited failures resolved would bypass the evidence boundary.
- Correction / current status: Proposal, actual decision, future ownership and fresh qualification gates remain separate; inherited tests/failures keep original attribution.
- Failure Intelligence: Retain the design/triage disposition, not invented implementation results or incidents.

### A2 launch-method coordination differed from the interim account

- What happened or could happen: The previously approved terminal fallback actually launched A2 before the later Sky-only correction arrived; an interim coordination message had believed the correction arrived first.
- Actual impact: A2 cannot be reported as global-Sky-only from launch. Subsequent UI used global Sky and the process stopped before input registration on the separate provider failure.
- Potential impact: Keeping the interim account would conceal the actual method and make later capability comparisons misleading.
- Correction / current status: The final outcome and this record preserve actual order; A3 used its own reviewed global-key-only route. No app defect or security change is attributed to this coordination mismatch.
- Failure Intelligence: Retained here and in the native outcome/receipt chain; no separate source-defect incident is warranted.

### Initial audit ctime equality expectation was too strict

- What happened or could happen: The first audit interpretation treated the pre-cleanup journal ctime as necessarily equal to the current post-cleanup output ctime.
- Actual impact: Final review found a 14.153528 ms ctime difference, with device/inode/size/mtime/full hash matching; it corrected the expectation against the actual fresh-identity OutputAccess path. Source and output bytes were not changed to satisfy the audit.
- Potential impact: Ignoring the difference or weakening all identity checks would each obscure what the saved journal actually proves.
- Correction / current status: Both timestamps and the unchanged fields are retained; no full-stamp equality or restorable ephemeral grant is claimed. The final audit passed within that explicit boundary.
- Failure Intelligence: The mistaken audit expectation and correction are recorded in the final audit and this session, not invented as a production mutation incident.

### Successful Linux reveal displayed Windows-specific feedback

- What happened or could happen: The original shared renderer named Windows after A3's actual Linux/Thunar reveal.
- Actual impact: The file-manager action succeeded, but its visible explanation named the wrong platform.
- Potential impact: Misleading wording could make a correct cross-platform output action appear misrouted.
- Correction / current status: Only the string and its regression tests changed; independent renderer 67/67 and final integrated 87/87 passed. No native rerun of the repaired string occurred. The separate patch-risk artifact is provisional because its schema/storage/validator resources were unavailable, not a formal scan.
- Failure Intelligence: The actual defect, expected red and verified narrow source repair are preserved in the execution/receipt chain and this session; no separate incident document is required for this bounded wording issue.

**A2 and any further qualification:** High for the recorded A2 capability-block/closure and A3 bounded saved-output checks; actual visual states are explicitly attributed to CUA/operator observation. The lead rechecked source/output before integrating only the reviewed two-file repair, which passed 87/87; visual Thunar/picker assertions remain operator observations rather than claimed independent image replay.

**Not higher because:** A1 lacks a retained native canceled/filePaths result and standalone screenshot archive; native/outer timestamps are not synchronized, and saved hashes are not atomic loaded-image attestation. ## 🎯 Session Goal

The preceding checkpoint deliberately stopped short of a claim that the changed ordinary service worked with real media. It had new three-case calibration on the corrected producer source, a reviewed capsule bound to that exact raw report and passing pure gates. It did not yet exercise actual service publication, a newly appearing output collision, active encoding cancellation or fresh-instance recovery on the post-fix source. Those gaps could not be closed by the older five-case service proof because that proof belonged to an earlier source epoch.

The goal was therefore one bounded real normal-service campaign on immutable checkpoint 012, followed by independent saved-output review and honest preservation. It was not to change code, broaden geometry/audio/defaults, activate the candidate for users or bypass the native workbench. An additional evidence goal was explicit from the start: retain diagnostics for every actual child so the earlier calibration's successful non-collector stderr gap would not be repeated.

The session aimed to:

- Preserve the new source/capsule checkpoint in the established Git → verified Notion → verified original-ancestry GitHub workflow, recording the actual asynchronous timing.
- Exercise three real publications, a sentinel collision introduced after preparation, observed live-encoding cancellation and recovery only after drainage/closure with a new handle and new service.
- Retain actual child stderr, total-byte/truncation metadata, invocation identity and closure, including cancellation errors, rather than interpreting empty outer stderr as all-child cleanliness.
- Independently audit the saved evidence and exact source/runtime/report binding before describing the service step as established.
- Prepare the next native journey without claiming that preparation, runtime restoration, a GUI launch or native acceptance has already happened.

The intended stopping point is a source-bound, audited bounded service proof ready for preservation and a separately reviewed native plan. Same-service recovery, evidence-phase cancellation, general media behavior and production acceptance remain outside that target.

### 4. Observe no-overwrite after preparation and real active-encoding cancellation

For the collision case, the sentinel appeared after preparation at the expected destination. The service reported `skipped` with “Output appeared since preview.” The retained sentinel SHA-256 remained `541e7f3289bcf1fd9b36608330013a775380c53fbf579763a9c3e0445b8368b2`. The progress-bearing child count was 14 before and 14 after this attempt. The observation establishes this after-prepare collision refusal, not a claim that no inspection or other work could ever occur in a skipped case.

The cancellation case waited for actual live encoding. The observed record shows `childLive: true`, frame 7, encoded time 0.149333 seconds, stage `encoding` and `ended: false`. Child 75 ultimately closed with `SIGKILL` and runner code `CANCELLED`; this is not a graceful termination claim. Cancellation therefore did not accidentally test an already completed job or only an idle service. The item became `cancelled`, reason code `CANCELLED`, with no published destination from that attempt.

The incomplete temporary artifact was retained for safety because it was never identity-bound. The report explicitly says the temporary directory was retained. It would be wrong to turn “no published output” into “all temporary files removed” or to claim cleanup erased material that the safety policy intentionally preserved. The retained interrupted child diagnostics are discussed below.

After cancellation, the old session was drained and closed. The report records two closure states with admission closed, not busy and empty closure-error lists. Recovery was then performed with a new runtime handle and a new service, producing the third publication. `sameServiceRetryPermitted` and the overall `sameServiceRecoveryProven` remain false. This bounded run does not prove same-instance recovery, evidence-phase cancellation or arbitrary repeated cancellation under overlapping work.

### 5. Close the entire child set and retain complete diagnostic metadata

The campaign spawned and closed 119 children, maximum one live concurrently and final live count zero. `census.encodes` equals 28 because it counts every progress-bearing child, including capability/other progress invocations; it is not the number of published outputs or target jobs. The owned artifact census reports 88,526,391 bytes at close and 290 retained artifact entries. This is the recorded pre-report close inventory, not an invented total after all later audit/portable documents were added.

Every one of the 119 child entries has known stderr capture, with zero marked truncated. Of those captures, 103 are empty and 16 nonempty. The outer stderr remains empty, but the per-child records make its limited meaning visible. Unlike the preceding calibration, successful non-collector diagnostics are not assumed missing or clean: their actual retained content is available.

The three successful target encodes each contain 499 bytes of stderr: duplicate codec-option warnings for HEVC/FLAC, x265 “Failed to genrate CPU mask” and no-thread-pool warnings for WPP/lookahead slices. The cancelled target child contains 1,232 bytes, including those messages plus interrupted demux/H.264 packet/NAL/partial-file diagnostics. Its termination is part of the observed active-cancel case, not a clean successful encode exit.

The remaining nonempty captures are capability/probe invocations in the two service instances: four H.264 codec-option warnings at 167 bytes, four H.265 codec-option/CPU-mask/thread-pool captures at 335 bytes and four SVT-AV1 captures at 1,181/1,182 bytes containing codec-option messages and encoder informational output. Across the complete census, all 16 nonempty children have codec-option messages and eight have CPU-mask/thread-pool diagnostics. Their presence does not activate AV1 or broaden the claimed H.265/FLAC service family. Nonempty stderr is reported as its actual content, not automatically equated with failed output and not discarded because exit was zero.

The old read-only warning diagnosis remains bounded: the global-copy/specific-codec argv pattern explains the duplicate codec selection messages under inspected FFmpeg handling, while missing topology visibility is a strong mechanism for the x265/libnuma issue but not independently proven as the sole cause. This run establishes recurrence of the diagnostics with full capture. It does not establish that native threading/performance is healthy or provide the missing exact-source/child-trace causal proof. No warning suppression, thread-pool workaround, sysfs change or runtime repin was used.

The outer completion receipt is 338 bytes, SHA-256 `6848b0715587a7bda50babba287b94e234b30baa7825ed2f58f0d7a6f393c8d1`. The compact completion receipt is 485 bytes, SHA-256 `febfc4294ce8cc3706cb3c290f7c7d2d153d1c411e7d674de0d71799b958ca28`. The raw report records 527 post-close source hashes checked and post-close identity verification true. Independent verification is recorded separately, not inferred solely from those producer assertions.

Its disk checks/owned-size allowance and supervisory abort request also are not continuous-write or hard-exit caps.

The plan forbids security-relaxing flags, guessed display values, loader overrides, injected IPC/devtools, setuid/chmod workarounds and quiet warning suppression. A sandbox/display/identity failure is a stopping observation, not permission to bypass it. An independent plan GO is preparation review, not execution authorization or live desktop readiness evidence.

## 🧠 Key Insights

### Actual consumer execution closes a different gap from calibration

The fresh calibration established the producer/evidence path on the corrected source; this campaign exercised actual ordinary service preparation, output publication, collision refusal, observed active cancellation and fresh-instance recovery using the newly bound capsule. That is a material advance over pure validators and injected factory tests. It still does not establish native renderer behavior or general release quality. The value is in naming the additional path actually tested, not broadening the claim until the distinction disappears.

### Better diagnostic retention can strengthen evidence without producing clean stderr

The deliberate capture improvement did not make warnings vanish. It made them countable and inspectable across all 119 children: 103 empty, 16 nonempty, none unknown or truncated. This is better evidence precisely because the result includes the target x265/codec warnings and interruption diagnostics rather than letting an empty outer stderr file hide them. A successful bounded output proof and unresolved diagnostic/performance uncertainty can both be true.

### No publication and no retained artifacts are different cancellation outcomes

The cancelled attempt did not publish the destination, but its incomplete temporary artifact remained because it lacked the identity binding required for safe deletion. The intended safety property is not “clean up everything at any cost.” It is refusal to publish incomplete output, drainage/closure and conservative treatment of unbound temporary files. Recovery was proven in a new owner/service, not by reviving the cancelled one.

### Preservation and execution have separate clocks

The local/Notion source gate preceded the campaign, while original-ancestry GitHub verification completed afterward. The later source receipt does not retroactively establish a service outcome before it happened, nor does it make the raw local report remotely backed up. Keeping those clocks separate prevents a plausible but inaccurate narrative of what was recoverable and tested at each point.

## 📌 Decisions Made

- Confirmed — The engineering lead authorized one bounded five-case service campaign only after the immutable 012 source, verified Notion restore and independent launcher gates. The earlier prepared-only status remains unchanged in its historical entry.
- Confirmed — The execution used the actual newly bound candidate and ordinary service path with existing resource/telemetry/storage/PCM/no-overwrite criteria. No source/default/catalog/runtime changes were bundled with the proof.
- Confirmed — All actual child stderr and byte/truncation metadata were retained, including successful warnings and cancellation errors. The lead's evidence standard is not satisfied by empty outer stderr alone.
- Confirmed — Recovery uses a new runtime handle and new service after old drainage/closure. The incomplete temporary artifact is retained where safety identity is absent; same-service recovery remains outside the accepted claim.
- Confirmed — After the independent saved-output audit passed and all 527 source files were rechecked, the engineering lead released documentation/evidence only. The accepted scope is the bounded five-case service result with its saved-record limits, not native execution or production clearance.
- Confirmed — Source tests retain their prior checkpoint 012 attribution and failures; no rerun or all-green claim is invented for this evidence-only session.
- Deferred — The native journey remains a separately reviewed bounded step. Preparation alone does not authorize restoration, a workbench launch, sandbox changes or native media.
- Deferred — Production/default/catalog activation, general geometry/audio/container coverage, GPU/Windows acceptance, packaging and owner release acceptance remain outside this service proof.

## ⚠️ Difficulties / Friction

The active-cancellation case had to observe a genuinely live encoder before requesting cancellation. A completed or idle job would not answer the intended question. This run captured frame 7 and an unfinished live child, so the bounded case was exercised without retry; that does not establish every later cancellation phase or timing window.

The retained warnings are meaningful friction, not cosmetic noise. All three successful target encodes again emitted the codec/x265 messages, and the cancelled child emitted additional interrupted-input diagnostics. The scoped functional result can stand while exact native causality and performance remain unresolved. Suppressing stderr or altering pools/topology would have changed the question instead of answering it.

Cancellation deliberately retained an unbound temporary artifact. That can look less tidy than deletion, but it is the observed conservative behavior and must be documented accurately. The next native experience will still need clear user-visible handling of cancellation/relaunch; this headless service proof does not establish what the renderer shows.

Raw reports, media and runtime prerequisites remain local. Readable portable preservation is useful, but exact path-bound/runtime authority cannot be recreated from that semantic copy. The next source mirror must preserve the full report meaning and current limits without promising that another machine can immediately launch the candidate.

The native boundary is still real despite a supported desktop and a prior successful version command. No window, sandbox, native output access or real UI journey was exercised in this campaign. The project should move to a bounded native check after preservation, not call the app usable because the service layer now has a successful run.

## 🔧 Adjustments Made

- Shifted from: Fresh calibration/capsule plus pure/injected positive checks, with actual post-fix service behavior unproven.
  - To: One actual source-bound five-case normal-service campaign with three publications, after-prepare collision refusal, active encoding cancellation and fresh-instance recovery.
  - Because: The completed source checkpoint and explicit one-attempt gate allowed the next missing consumer path to be exercised.
  - Consequence: The record can now claim the bounded actual service journey, but not native renderer acceptance or general product rollout.

- Shifted from: The calibration harness's missing successful non-collector stderr evidence.
  - To: Known, untruncated diagnostic capture for all 119 actual service-campaign children.
  - Because: The next launcher was deliberately prepared to retain stderr and byte/truncation metadata for every invocation, including errors.
  - Consequence: The 16 nonempty captures and recurring target warnings are explicit; the evidence gap narrows without falsely clearing the warnings.

## ❌ Mistakes / Risks Noted

### Successful output could be misreported as warning-free execution

- What happened or could happen: Actual three successful target encodes retained 499-byte warnings each, and 16 of 119 child stderr captures were nonempty. A reporting risk is collapsing this to the empty outer stderr result.
- Actual impact: The diagnostics recur and remain available for inspection. No failed publication is established for the three successful outputs.
- Potential impact: Hiding the warnings could overstate native threading/performance or obscure a later reliability issue.
- Correction / current status: Every capture is known and untruncated; the exact census and warning categories are retained. No suppression, topology change or causal clearance is claimed.
- Failure Intelligence: Retain recurrence and capture completeness in the linked execution/receipts and this session. The earlier warning diagnosis remains the reference; no duplicate incident is needed merely to repeat it.

### Active cancellation does not establish universal cleanup or same-service recovery

- What happened or could happen: Actual live encoding cancellation left no published output but retained the incomplete temporary artifact because it was not identity-bound. Recovery used a new handle and new service.
- Actual impact: The intended destination was not published; the temporary artifact remains. Both owned sessions closed with no reported closure errors.
- Potential impact: Describing this as “all files cleaned” or “retry works” could lead to unsafe deletion or reuse of a closed owner.
- Correction / current status: The record preserves retained-artifact status, observed live frame/time and explicit fresh-instance recovery. Same-service and evidence-phase cancellation remain unproved.
- Failure Intelligence: This is expected conservative behavior and a bounded coverage limit, not a newly established cleanup defect; retain it with the actual case evidence.

### The 28 progress-bearing invocations are not 28 published outputs

- What happened or could happen: Actual `census.encodes` includes every progress-bearing child, including capability work. The field name could be mistaken for a target-publication total.
- Actual impact: There are three successful publications, one collision skip and one cancelled target case; total children are 119.
- Potential impact: Inflated output/test counts would make the evidence appear broader than it is.
- Correction / current status: The summary and receipts distinguish progress-bearing child count, target cases and actual publications.
- Failure Intelligence: A reporting distinction is sufficient; no separate incident file is warranted.

### Saved-output review and portable preservation have finite authority

- What happened or could happen: A risk is treating saved-record checks as a second decode or treating a transformed portable report as original raw/runtime authority.
- Actual impact: Independent review rehashed saved artifacts/diagnostics and checked retained assertions; no independent decode occurred. The portable record transforms 284 root strings and excludes the inverse mapping.
- Potential impact: Applicability, byte restoration and cryptographic chronology could be overstated, or a later source-only restore incorrectly called runnable.
- Correction / current status: The audit explicitly limits chronology/loaded-image/private-handle claims. The portable report preserves complete semantics and warnings while denying raw restoration, factory substitution and activation authority.
- Failure Intelligence: Audit/portable limits belong in the execution/receipt and continuity records, not a fabricated lost-evidence incident.

### Prior regression failures and unrun native behavior remain separate limitations

- What happened or could happen: The actual source-test baseline still contains the old affected mismatch and 27 broad failures; no new unit/regression suite was executed for this evidence-only step. Native work remains planned.
- Actual impact: A bounded service result is available, but an all-green suite or usable native app is not established.
- Potential impact: Treating this milestone as release completion could skip unresolved source/platform checks or acceptance.
- Correction / current status: Prior tests retain their original checkpoint attribution; native/main-renderer behavior, performance, broader operation families and release acceptance remain gated.
- Failure Intelligence: Retain existing baseline and platform limitations in current records; this successful campaign does not create or erase their historical incidents.

### Resource admission is not a hard active-write or process-exit cap

- What happened or could happen: Existing bounded controls check free space/owned-size before work and request abort after a limit; they cannot guarantee active-write size or final process exit time.
- Actual impact: This attempt completed in 53,559 ms, below its configured envelope, with all 119 children closed and no live children.
- Potential impact: A different future run could exceed an admission-time forecast or take longer to drain after abort.
- Correction / current status: Keep the precise controls/limits and require independent review of any new native campaign rather than carrying over a hard-guarantee claim.
- Failure Intelligence: Known operating limitation, not an observed runaway or disk-exhaustion event here.

- Complete checkpoint 011's established Git → verified Notion → original-ancestry GitHub preservation and retain the actual overlap with later calibration.

## 🧠 Key Insights

### Fresh evidence, not permissive repinning, closes the old source gate

The exact-source gate worked in both directions. It rejected the preparation correction under old evidence, and a new campaign/audit gave a concrete basis for admitting the corrected producer epoch. Treating that temporary red state as an inconvenience to remove would have erased the reason for the gate. Treating all future use as blocked forever would also have missed the intended recovery path: fresh evidence, explicit review and a new capsule.

### A shared import closure is not an executed consumer journey

Calibration and consumer execution answer different questions even when they share a large import closure. The new run shows the established calibration producer and exact runtime can still satisfy the narrow KeepSource720 timing/PCM/resource checks on the corrected source. It does not show the updated normal `EncoderService` lifecycle, output issuance, collision/cancellation/recovery journey or native workbench under actual media. The capsule update is a necessary bridge to that test, not the test itself.

### The saved fields determine what can be claimed

Evidence retention determines the claim. Twenty-one clean collector stderr records support exactly twenty-one clean collector observations. A clean outer process and successful output do not erase unretained child diagnostics or earlier target-encode warnings. Likewise, a full hash first computed during audit is useful present integrity evidence but not proof that the same full hash was captured before the event.

### Preservation and preflight each establish a bounded prerequisite

The preservation sequence is operational rather than ceremonial. The full tracked-source gate kept the 338,107-byte historical summary stable while media was running; the later capsule and documentation belong to another checkpoint. Exact Notion restoration, a complete named-branch bundle and verified original-ancestry GitHub bytes each establish a different recoverability fact. None restores ignored runtime/media automatically.

A version preflight is a useful small advance but still a small one. It narrows the uncertainty from “downloaded executable not invoked” to “version path loaded and reported its identity with a D-Bus warning.” It does not answer sandbox, display, native child ownership or app usability questions, so it should not be described as Electron/workbench acceptance.

- Confirmed — The implementation and lead review kept geometry, CPU tuple, runtime identity, seven listed dependencies, resource policy, bounds and historical/default/catalog behavior unchanged. No diagnostic-suppression or environment/security change was included.
- Confirmed — The lead retained the affected diagnostic mismatch and all 27 broad baseline failures. Eight stale-producer/factory refusals disappeared only after the fresh binding; that count change is not an all-green regression claim.
- Confirmed — Lead-owned portable evidence is semantic-only, with eight prefix substitutions and explicit denial of raw restoration/factory substitution. Its preservation does not establish that ignored runtime/media/raw files have been backed up.
- Confirmed — The later bounded Electron version preflight is recorded as loading/version evidence with retained D-Bus stderr. Its actual result does not revise checkpoint 011's earlier acquisition-only cutoff or establish app acceptance.

## ⚠️ Difficulties / Friction

The first difficulty was structural: fixing shared preparation behavior deliberately invalidated the old evidence closure. Requalification therefore required more than a small test update. The completed single campaign and audit supplied that missing basis without disguising the cost or rewriting the 011 refusal result.

Diagnostic retention is incomplete for successful non-collector children. The final audit explicitly narrowed the claim, but the lack of those records means this run cannot settle the previous target-codec/x265 warning question. It would be misleading to infer that the missing warnings vanished just because the outer stderr file is empty.

The affected suite remains intermittently sensitive to the old missing-source diagnostic distinction. Its failure is not newly explained or fixed by the capsule work. The final 283/284 receipt is stronger evidence for the scoped update than an invented all-green summary, and it leaves the old mismatch visible for later investigation.

The new normal-service proof still needs its own source-preserved launch gate. Pure validation and injected factory tests make preparation safer, but no actual post-fix service publication/cancellation/recovery result exists yet. Native work remains another stage beyond that, despite the executable version preflight succeeding.

Large cumulative records and multiple preservation layers create opportunities for accidental historical drift. The mitigation here is an outside draft, exact prior-body preservation, explicit ownership, separate source/report epochs and verification before release. The original summary correction receipt and older archives are not regenerated as a side effect of adding this session.

## ❌ Mistakes / Risks Noted

### Successful non-collector stderr was not individually retained

- What happened or could happen: Actual evidence-retention limitation; the prepared plan's generic child-stderr wording did not establish what every successful child saved.
- Actual impact: The fresh calibration cannot settle whether the earlier target-codec/x265 warnings recurred. No resulting output failure is established.
- Potential impact: Calling empty outer stderr an all-child clean result could hide diagnostic or native-performance uncertainty.
- Correction / current status: Final audit records 21 clean, untruncated collector stderr streams and separately marks other successful-child stderr unretained. Prior warnings stay open; the next prepared service launcher explicitly captures invocation diagnostics.
- Failure Intelligence: Retained in this session and execution/receipts as a coverage limitation. No duplicate standalone incident file is needed.

### The intermittent missing-source assertion and broad baseline failures remain

- What happened or could happen: Actual worker 158/159 and lead 283/284 affected results retain actual PROTECTED_SOURCE versus expected SOURCE_CHECK_INCOMPLETE; broad 181/208 retains 27 failures.
- Actual impact: Affected and broad test processes exit 1. Eight gate-dependent refusals resolve, but the old baseline is not green.
- Potential impact: A passing repeat or count improvement could be mistaken for fixing the old diagnostic divergence or removing all regressions.
- Correction / current status: Exact results and normalized 27-name comparison are preserved. No expectations or production source were changed to eliminate the old mismatch.
- Failure Intelligence: Keep the existing baseline/failure history and this result in the linked execution/receipt record; no newly diagnosed production defect is asserted.

### A fresh capsule could be confused with arbitrary repinning or consumer proof

- What happened or could happen: Potential authority/coverage error, not the method adopted. The calibration producer and normal-service consumer remain different execution paths.
- Actual impact: None established: source remained frozen through calibration/audit, and the one-file capsule was changed only after release.
- Potential impact: Accepting current hashes without fresh evidence, or treating calibration as real service/native execution, would overextend qualification.
- Correction / current status: Fresh actual report/audit and complete 97-entry closure underpin the new binding. Real post-fix normal-service and native work are still unrun.
- Failure Intelligence: Record the protected boundary here; no incident file for a shortcut that was not taken.

### Saved-output integrity and chronology have finite scope

- What happened or could happen: Potential overclaim if a saved-byte audit is described as a second decode or all full hashes as pre-event captures.
- Actual impact: No independent re-decode occurred; some complete output/telemetry hashes were first established during audit.
- Potential impact: Overstating historical hash baselines or timestamp authority would make evidence appear stronger than it is.
- Correction / current status: The record separates saved identity/spool checks, newly established full hashes, retained digest assertions and control-flow-based chronology.
- Failure Intelligence: Audit limitations are already explicit in the receipt and this session; no additional incident record is warranted.

### Portable/source preservation does not restore live raw authority

- What happened or could happen: Potential restoration error if the transformed portable report or source backup is used as the original factory input.
- Actual impact: Exact runtime/media/raw files remain local evidence, outside the source backup; no lost artifact is established here.
- Potential impact: A future restore could be reported runnable despite lacking the exact ordinary raw report, runtime pair or listed dependencies.
- Correction / current status: The portable record denies raw restoration and factory substitution; exact original 55,953-byte report/hash are recorded separately.
- Failure Intelligence: Track as a continuation/restoration prerequisite in current and execution records, not a fabricated loss incident.

### Electron version success is not native acceptance

- What happened or could happen: Potential platform overclaim. Actual preflight returned v44.7.0 / exit 0 with 161-byte missing-D-Bus stderr.
- Actual impact: Version-only loading succeeded; no workbench, sandboxed renderer or actual GUI/media journey was attempted.
- Potential impact: Treating this as app acceptance could skip security/display/ownership failures not exercised by --version.
- Correction / current status: Diagnostic retained; no workaround or security-relaxing flag. Native journey remains separately gated.
- Failure Intelligence: The preflight receipt and this limitation suffice; no native failure incident is inferred from an unrun app.

### Abort and disk admission are not hard enforcement

- What happened or could happen: Existing operational limit, not an observed runaway in this attempt.
- Actual impact: The one campaign completed in 201,431 ms with 42 children closed / max 1 / live 0 and no finalization error.
- Potential impact: A future child could exceed an abort request or consume space after a pre-child admission check.
- Correction / current status: Keep 30-minute abort-request and 27 GiB admission wording, plus explicit absence of hard active-write/exit caps; next campaign needs its own bounded review.
- Failure Intelligence: Retain as a known planning limit; no runaway or disk-exhaustion incident occurred here.

### Pending 012 preservation must not be described as complete

- What happened or could happen: Potential continuity/reporting error. Only 011 preservation is complete at this summary cutoff.
- Actual impact: The new capsule/evidence/session are local working records awaiting coherent checkpoint and mirror/publication.
- Potential impact: Readers could mistake a saved document or planned send for verified remote recoverability.
- Correction / current status: Git → verified Notion → verified original-ancestry GitHub remains next work, with own actual receipts. Prior 338,107-byte history stays exact.
- Failure Intelligence: Status/ownership controls in current records suffice; no failed publication is alleged.

No new production lifecycle defect was established by this capsule-only work; that is not a prediction that later service/native tests cannot find one. Checkpoint 011's actual real-await and clock-comparison failure history remains exact, rather than being rewritten as part of requalification.

**Why:** The campaign is tied to immutable `77331ff` with all 524 tracked hashes frozen, exact runtime/dependency identities, exclusive attempt markers, inner/outer completion records, three real output cases and a closed-child census. Independent saved-output review rechecked retained source/output/spool bytes, PCM/timing/resource records and the unchanged old report. The capsule diff is one file, backed by fresh report/audit hashes and a complete 97-entry producer closure; independent and final lead tests retain their actual counts and failures. The prior 338,107-byte summary is preserved exactly rather than summarized away.

**Not higher because:** Saved-output audit is not independent re-decoding or cryptographic chronology attestation; some full hashes were first established during audit and raw stream bytes are not all retained. Successful non-collector stderr is missing, so earlier target warnings remain unresolved. The normal-service consumer has not run on this new source/capsule, positive factory tests are injected, native sandbox/workbench behavior is untested, and source backup does not restore ignored runtime/raw/media prerequisites. The old diagnostic mismatch and 27 broad failures remain. No production, general geometry, GPU, Windows or same-service recovery claim is supported.

## 🎯 Session Goal

Checkpoint 010 intentionally left an important distinction visible: its pending-write and stale-object tests modeled states but did not execute the actual base `prepareItem` → `recordIncident` await or a captured delayed inspection callback. The next work was to test those real boundaries before describing the developer workbench as natively ready. It was not initially a plan to loosen producer gates, replace the runtime or rerun successful media.

The first added test exposed a cancelled initial-preparation row still labelled ready even though the plan was already null. That observation needed a narrow interpretation: it was misleading presentation, not proof of runnable authority or a completed unwanted encode. Keeping the correct non-runnable assertions allowed the real callback tests to continue. A separate held final-receipt test then found a more consequential state: `busy: false`, phase ready and a live plan while `PREPARATION_FINISHED` persistence was still pending. That new finding, and review of related failure-receipt paths, expanded the session into a bounded shared-service correction.

The session aimed to:

- Exercise the actual inspection/preparation receipt await and captured stale callbacks, with cancellation/drainage and new-operation ownership visible.
- Preserve admission, failure precedence and original batch/plan correlation through success and failure receipt writes.
- Correct only the shared lifecycle and its tests, retain all red evidence and distinguish an intermittent baseline diagnostic mismatch from a resolved defect.
- Keep exact old candidate producer pins unchanged, explicitly stop candidate use on changed source and prepare the next honest requalification boundary.

Later desktop discovery and pinned Electron acquisition advanced one native prerequisite in parallel. Those later observations did not undo the source requalification requirement or establish a running application.

## 🛠️ Actions Taken

### 1. Anchor the source to checkpoint 010 and its actual preservation chronology

Checkpoint 010 is local `1af9c682bda254cab04ec4e7d1ed88b76a5d9f61`. Its [Notion checkpoint](https://app.notion.com/p/41bbd7ade63b81fc81adc3eadf2cc5ec) restored all 522 source files and cloned the complete ten-commit local-history bundle. The source archive SHA-256 is `cbf5f81e3f7a82a39146175cd4344d0c91e8aaff5bacf7167f2ff220a289aa0d`; the bundle SHA-256 is `4c38133c0912bf0b381a8f61c1f79697c5d7e157e40cea7c7ccff7d99d633a56`. The 293,033-byte summary SHA-256 is `8f3904745d0ea49b69908bc2e09a8c0071b1b36cc4a31710abab4569c4167f9c`, preserved below as the exact prior body.

GitHub publication was verified at 16:51:04 UTC as `c2046b05e5cb7ba495e15dcfe88f3bcb441ad814`, sole parent `8685c2f3d51e89c24fb3ec5ab8d549978172017a`, on `CipherVaults/FFmpeg-Encoder-Blue-Linux`, branch `blue/linux-development`. This backup retains the old workbench/source and its exact existing gates. It does not make the later changed shared service qualified, and it does not turn the earlier media evidence into a test of the new source.

### 2. Traverse the real FILE_INSPECTED await and interpret the initial red result correctly

The existing service baseline at `20261117T165006Z_real-file-inspected-lifecycle` recorded 75 passes and one failure out of 76. The failure was the known missing-source diagnostic mismatch: actual `PROTECTED_SOURCE` versus expected `SOURCE_CHECK_INCOMPLETE`. That baseline failure was kept visible before new assertions were added.

The new tests call actual initial preparation or `updateFileOperations` draft repreparation. They intercept the real `recordIncident` call for `FILE_INSPECTED`, hold it on a controlled promise and capture the actual inspection `onStage` callback. While the receipt is held, they check busy admission, refusal of Start/new preparation, and that both the preparation and cancellation promises remain unsettled until release. After drain, they check null plan, refused stale Start, zero fixture media calls and stale-callback effects after cancellation and during a later preparation.

The first added run, `20261117T165033Z_real-file-inspected-lifecycle-added`, recorded 76 passes/two failures of 78. One was the unchanged baseline mismatch. The new initial-preparation test failed because a row still had `ready` status after cancellation, although the phase was cancelled and plan/plan ID were null. The draft path passed. The initial test stopped at that row assertion, so its later stale-callback steps were not established by that red run.

The lead preserved the authoritative assertions and treated the ready-row result as a presentation inconsistency rather than claiming an encode or authority escape had occurred. With that distinction made, the real captured callback checks could run: an old callback did not change state or emit updates after cancellation or while a fresh preparation owned the service. These are real shared-service control-flow/callback tests with fixture inspection and no media calls, not a native job or a previously invented disconnected old-item example.

### 3. Reproduce a distinct admission window at the actual final receipt

A separate test held the real initial `PREPARATION_FINISHED` write. Receipt `20261117T165224Z_real-initial-final-receipt-diagnostic` recorded 77 passes/two failures out of 79, including the baseline mismatch and the new final-receipt assertion. Its captured diagnostic was `busy: false`, `phase: ready`, `planPresent: true` while the receipt promise was still held.

That state differs materially from the earlier cancelled/null-plan presentation issue: the final write had not drained, yet the shared service's phase no longer kept preparation admission busy. The red test failed at its first busy assertion. It did **not** proceed to execute Start, overlap another preparation or edit the plan in that run. The record therefore establishes the observed admission window, not an observed overlapping encode, corrupted journal or destructive effect.

Independent review also found the related failure-receipt windows: initial and draft paths set a terminal failed/cancelled phase before their `PREPARATION_FAILED` or `DRAFT_REPREPARATION_FAILED` write completed. The correction had to cover those awaits as well as the final success receipt, rather than only turn the single red assertion green.

### 4. Reuse the existing finish helper and capture operation correlation

Only `src/encoder-service.cjs` and `tests/encoder-service.test.cjs` changed. The initial reviewed patch was 18,016 bytes, SHA-256 `614d0c0a5e23bf10f403b051a8cf20f69e876524558a9e95daa654dbac7226f1`. The subsequent V2 test-only clock comparison correction produces an 18,195-byte patch, SHA-256 `0bd20ede6f98d94aaf021c817c67d753b3ebe774a639938b834d3f512eb8ac7b`; the production service hash remains unchanged. Its final independent disposition is recorded below. The source reuses the existing `finishPreparationReceipt` mechanism rather than introducing another admission subsystem.

Initial preparation now captures its batch/plan receipt IDs before the asynchronous work and uses those captured IDs for started, failed and finished records. The final receipt goes through the helper, which keeps the externally busy phase at preparing while persistence is awaited. It preserves the intended terminal phase in the record context and only exposes the final state after receipt handling. The optional message parameter preserves the initial failure text; shared callers that do not supply it retain their existing behavior.

Both initial and draft failure-receipt paths also keep phase preparing while the failure diagnostic is written, then restore the computed terminal phase before the final helper runs. Failure precedence remains stronger than late cancellation: an inspection infrastructure failure is not rewritten as a successful cancellation merely because cancellation arrives during its receipt.

When cancellation arrives during the final write, the reused helper clears plan/draft authority and per-item re-encode authorization, invalidates relevant operation authorities and changes eligible rows to cancelled. If a finished record already describes ready, a later `PREPARED_AUTHORITY_REVOKED` record explains the cancellation using the same captured batch/plan IDs and a later sequence. The old ready record is not silently rewritten. This also removes the earlier cancelled initial row's misleading ready presentation as part of the shared finalization path.

The six added tests cover two real `FILE_INSPECTED` await paths, initial successful final-receipt drainage, initial failed final-receipt precedence/correlation, and both initial/draft failure-receipt admission windows. They use actual service entry points and actual captured callbacks while controlling journal completion and inspection fixtures. On corrected source they assert busy Start/new-prepare/edit refusal while held, pending cancellation until drain, correct terminal/null-authority state, preserved correlation and receipt order, ignored stale callbacks and no fixture media calls.

## 🧠 Key Insights

### Receipt persistence is part of the preparation lifetime

The actual final-receipt test showed why setting phase ready before awaiting a journal write is not merely a cosmetic ordering choice. In this service, phase determines busy admission. A pending receipt therefore belongs inside the owned preparation lifetime; otherwise ready presentation and admission can appear before the operation has finished its awaited receipt write. Reusing the existing finalization helper makes that relationship explicit across initial and draft paths.

### A ready-looking row and a live ready plan are different observations

The first cancelled-row red result had no plan authority and did not demonstrate an encode. The second final-receipt red result retained a live plan with busy false, but stopped before executing overlapping actions. Keeping both distinctions prevents two opposite errors: dismissing a genuine admission window as a visual issue, or overstating a visual issue and first assertion as observed destructive execution.

### A later revocation record can preserve truthful history better than editing the first receipt

Cancellation may arrive while a finished receipt describing ready is being written. Captured IDs and an ordered revocation record preserve what was recorded and what changed afterward. Rewriting the ready receipt or borrowing the next operation's IDs would conceal the boundary the tests are meant to establish. The corrected code and fixture assertions support correlation/order for these paths, not general crash-proof persistence.

### Exact evidence gates can make a safer source tree temporarily less usable

The shared lifecycle correction is useful, but its source identity no longer matches the historical candidate producer set. Eight additional broad refusals are therefore part of the honest result. Updating a hash map just to regain green tests would erase the relationship between proof and implementation. The correct continuation is to preserve this source and deliberately requalify it, not claim that old successful media already tested it.

### Environment discovery and acquisition are separate from operational readiness

The later desktop and acquired Electron resolve narrower questions than a successful native launch. They do not establish dynamic dependencies, sandbox behavior, exact candidate restoration or changed-source qualification. This progress is useful precisely because the remaining blockers are now separate and testable rather than compressed into either “no desktop” or “native ready.”

## 📌 Decisions Made

- **Confirmed by the lead:** Execute real awaited service/receipt paths rather than rely on checkpoint 010's modeled pending-write and disconnected-object tests.
- **Confirmed after the actual red observation:** Reuse `finishPreparationReceipt`, capture initial receipt IDs and keep both initial/draft failure writes in preparing state until they drain. Preserve failure precedence and ordered revocation on late cancellation.
- **Confirmed evidence treatment:** Retain the presentation-only first red, the actual final-receipt admission red and their assertion stop points. Do not claim an overlapping Start or media event occurred in a red run that stopped earlier.
- **Confirmed verification boundary:** Keep the intermittent missing-source diagnostic mismatch visible despite one worker 170/170 pass. Final lead 245/246 and broad 173/208 with 35 failures remain the measured outcomes.
- **Confirmed authority boundary:** Leave the old candidate producer map, pins and original report unchanged. The corrected source requires a new reviewed qualification step before candidate/native media, and the old proof remains attributed to its old source.
- **Completed authorized prerequisite only:** Discover the later desktop and acquire official pinned Electron 44.7.0 in isolated storage with byte verification. Do not equate acquisition with launch or change security controls to force it.
- **Deferred:** Actual native app/media execution, candidate restoration/requalification, same-service candidate recovery, general threading/performance, broader families, release acceptance and packaging. Ahmed retains acceptance; packaging stays last.

## ⚠️ Difficulties / Friction

The work required separating test-model gaps from actual source defects. Checkpoint 010 had already corrected inflated descriptions, but only executing the real await revealed which concern was merely a cancelled-row presentation issue and which left busy admission open. The first test's early assertion also meant its later callback checks could not be claimed until they actually ran.

The shared helper already handled parts of draft finalization, making it tempting to fix only the initial final write. Review identified failure-diagnostic awaits as separate windows in both initial and draft paths. The bounded correction therefore had to preserve terminal failure meaning and operation correlation while holding admission, without introducing a second scheduler or rewriting old receipts.

Test totals are nonuniform for distinct reasons, and the independent repeat added a separate test-clock mistake. The missing-source diagnostic mismatch is a pre-existing intermittent result, seen before the patch, absent in one later worker run and present again in the lead affected run. Separately, eight new candidate-dependent refusals are expected consequences of the deliberately unchanged producer gate. Neither category should be hidden, combined into “only the old failures,” or treated as proof that every newly blocked positive test body executed. The independent elapsed-time comparison failure is different again: excluding only a derived clock field makes the new state assertion meaningful without changing production behavior or hiding a callback update.

The later desktop discovery changed the practical environment picture, but the terminal had different PATH/display visibility from earlier inspection. Using the existing absolute Node path established access to the same project; it did not authorize arbitrary setup or prove Electron. Pinned acquisition was completed under a bounded separate setup, while native launch and source qualification remain separate unresolved steps.

## 🔧 Adjustments Made

- Shifted from: Modeled pending-write emission and disconnected stale-object coverage in the previous source checkpoint.
  - To: Actual `FILE_INSPECTED`, final-receipt and failure-receipt awaits reached through shared service entry points, with real captured callbacks.
  - Because: The prior record explicitly left these exact paths untested.
  - Consequence: The session found both a presentation inconsistency and a distinct admission window, then added targeted regression coverage instead of inflating the previous tests.
- Shifted from: Initial preparation marking a terminal/ready phase before its diagnostic writes finished.
  - To: Preparing/busy admission through failure and final receipt persistence, using captured IDs and the existing helper's late-cancellation revocation.
  - Because: The held final receipt actually exposed busy false with a ready phase and live plan, and review found related failure windows.
  - Consequence: Corrected source retains lifetime/correlation until drain and changes eligible cancelled rows consistently, while old receipt history remains intact.
- Shifted from: A candidate composition whose exact old producer map still matched the workbench source.
  - To: Explicit fail-closed refusal on the changed shared service and a required new qualification step.
  - Because: This is a real producer-source change, not a documentation-only update.
  - Consequence: Broad failures increase from 27 to 35; no hash-map or raw-report rewrite is used to conceal the cost.
- Shifted from: The earlier scoped environment inspection without a found running Electron/display setup.
  - To: A later verified desktop/terminal and isolated, byte-verified Electron acquisition.
  - Because: The supported desktop surface exposed additional actual capabilities and the authorized pinned setup completed.
  - Consequence: One prerequisite is available, but executable launch, sandbox/dependency behavior and changed-source media qualification remain unproven. The earlier dated record is preserved.

- Shifted from: Whole-snapshot equality in the new captured-callback tests, including a derived elapsed clock.
  - To: Equality of all remaining state plus unchanged update-count assertions, excluding only `elapsedSeconds`.
  - Because: Independent repetition observed 0.005 versus 0.006 seconds with unchanged revision and other state, demonstrating time-sensitive test logic rather than a stale-callback mutation.
  - Consequence: The independent red remains, V2 is test-only and subsequent worker results still retain the unrelated baseline mismatch. No production callback behavior or other expectation was weakened.

## ❌ Mistakes / Risks Noted

- **Actual presentation inconsistency:** After initial cancellation, a row remained ready while the plan was null. Its observed impact was misleading state, not an encoded output. The corrected shared finalizer now clears authority and changes eligible rows; the original red evidence remains.
- **Actual admission-window observation:** While initial `PREPARATION_FINISHED` was held, busy was false and a ready live plan existed. The red test stopped at its first assertion, so overlapping actions were a potential consequence, not an observed event. The corrected test now exercises their busy refusal and cancellation drainage.
- **Actual test-time-sensitivity mistake, corrected in V2:** Whole-snapshot equality included derived `elapsedSeconds`, producing an independent red with only 0.005 versus 0.006 in the reported snapshot difference. The following update-count assertion was not reached in that red run; the later passing V2 cases establish that check. The fix omits only that clock field, retains all remaining state/update assertions and preserves the red log; production source is unchanged.
- **Actual baseline instability, unresolved:** A missing-source test intermittently returns `PROTECTED_SOURCE` instead of `SOURCE_CHECK_INCOMPLETE`. The patch did not change that production behavior, and a single 170/170 pass does not fix it. The lead's affected result retains the failure.
- **Intentional new integration blockage:** Eight candidate-dependent tests now refuse the changed producer, giving 35 broad failures. The candidate route is not presently usable on this source without fresh qualification. Calling this merely the old 27 failures would misstate current state.
- **Potential overclaim, avoided:** Real shared-service control flow still uses controlled inspection/journal/media fixtures. It is not a native process, actual encode, crash-recovery or universal concurrency proof. Acquired Electron bytes are not a successful sandboxed launch.
