## 🎯 Session Goal

The previous campaign had established a narrow genuine-service result: prepare/Start, three local range publications, collision protection, active cancellation and fresh-instance recovery. It did not connect that behavior to the existing visible developer workbench. The next useful step was a finite source integration using the current shell and theme, not a replacement interface, a production default change or another calibration run.

The initial design had to resolve two real mismatches. The workbench passed main-owned output access and expected Remux status, while the strict candidate factory did not yet support that integration shape. More importantly, the existing UI obtains per-file range controls after initial preparation, whereas the candidate does not qualify Full. Range cannot simply be inserted into batch defaults because the contract excludes it there. The goal was to retain inspection and editable per-file drafts without letting an unqualified initial compatibility row become Start authority.

The session aimed to:

- Add one explicit, fixed developer candidate choice while preserving baseline/AV1 routing, defaults, security and the existing interface.
- Derive restoration paths and exact runtime pins in trusted main code, preserving the original raw calibration and dependency gates without copying binaries or accepting renderer-supplied paths.
- Make inspection-only rows editable but not runnable, then reprepare an explicit per-file range through the existing draft/plan/family path.
- Make cancellation visibly require close/relaunch, test source-level admission/lifecycle behavior and record native and real-await gaps honestly.

Review later required narrower descriptions of two tests that modeled timing rather than traversing the claimed production await/callback path. Correcting those descriptions became part of the handoff; it was not a retroactive claim that the missing coverage had been implemented.

## 🛠️ Actions Taken

### 1. Establish the preserved parent and an independent unchanged baseline

Checkpoint 009 is local `dfa1658a2856cf1f021fdcdb50f1974fb60eaf43`. Its [Notion checkpoint](https://app.notion.com/p/41bbd7ade63b816bb6ece36db5ad3be7) restored all 517 source files and the complete nine-commit local bundle. The [full native summary page](https://app.notion.com/p/41bbd7ade63b81498255c76dd3ba66cc) was fetched unabridged, preserving the 255,403-byte cumulative summary. Its final committed SHA-256 is `82ddc4fb8f16df302e7a292a62097aa381d71197647e9d771b656fddd6d1ca94`; this exact committed body is the suffix preserved below.

GitHub preservation completed at 16:24:45 UTC as `8685c2f3d51e89c24fb3ec5ab8d549978172017a`, sole parent `14be3e1e218d2ea97cef10583b8d166ebd771e96`, on `CipherVaults/FFmpeg-Encoder-Blue-Linux`, branch `blue/linux-development`. The 899-file remote tree had 894 unchanged paths, two modified and three new, with no deletions. Changed bytes, ancestry, tree and final ref were verified without force. This is completed checkpoint 009 publication, not publication of the new integration.

Independent review extracted the exact 517-file parent ZIP and verified it against its manifest before running 125 UI/output-access/IPC/main-lifecycle baseline tests, all passing. Receipt `20261117T162737Z_candidate-workbench-parent009-ui` belongs to that immutable extraction. It was not a Git checkout, and its empty wrapper HEAD field is not evidence of an unknown source: the verified ZIP manifest and `workbench-baseline009-location.json` identify `dfa1658`. These were pure/injected checks, not Windows, Electron or native media execution.

### 2. Inspect the real UI preparation constraint before changing its semantics

The read-only design traced the actual workbench and draft code. First Prepare sends folder/settings information; per-file range editors appear after a draft is returned. The allowed batch-default contract excludes range. The earlier service launcher had prepared a default draft and then applied a per-file override, so it could not be cited as proof of a direct pre-prepare GUI range route.

Inspection established that existing unsupported draft rows remain editable: the per-file editor depends on an editable draft and file membership, not on being ready or selected for Start. The implementation therefore reused those controls. It did not invent a range, add range to batch defaults, qualify Full, change the shared `EncoderService`, widen IPC fields or introduce a new shell.

The reviewed solution keeps the existing candidate Start guard and adds a candidate-workbench-only snapshot boundary. Before an update snapshot is emitted, a nominally ready entry lacking the required qualification selection, runtime token or instrumentation, or still marked as a compatibility plan, loses its runnable plan entry and becomes unsupported with `CANDIDATE_RANGE_REQUIRED`. Its draft/source row remains available for explicit per-file editing. Full may be internally described during inspection; it is not thereby qualified or startable. The original Start guard already refuses the compatibility entry before the new snapshot demotion occurs.

### 3. Add fixed main-owned acquisition and explicit routing

The exact new option is `--blue-runtime=9.0.2-keep720-candidate`. With no runtime option, baseline remains selected, and the exact existing AV1 option remains available. Duplicate, unknown or path-valued runtime choices are rejected. There is no environment selector, arbitrary manifest URL, renderer executable path or silent fallback to an old factory.

The new main-owned `candidate-workbench.cjs` derives a fixed ignored restoration directory under `linux-blue/artifacts/candidate-runtime-1980b0c0480dba85e00fb69f6994e9fd022b46d6355538ee24a445c87da337b9/`, with fixed `ffmpeg` and `ffprobe` basenames. It records the public exact tool sizes/hashes and seven previously inspected dependency identities. No binary was copied, committed, downloaded or opened by this work. Those fixed restoration binaries are absent in the inspected workspace; the existence of separately retained earlier binaries does not satisfy this new acquisition path automatically.

The helper rejects unsuitable loader environments and checks required inputs before opening the candidate. The genuine runtime factory and existing original 55,953-byte raw long-calibration gate remain authoritative, including SHA-256 `1feb0f2958c78cc73683c70fed08835d1e61b98462e8b30e0127f95b5cf350ab` and the historical producer checks. A transformed portable report cannot satisfy that gate. Missing inputs produce an explicit unavailable error, not a false-ready window, unreviewed download or baseline fallback.

Main now selects the candidate helper only for that explicit option and uses a separate candidate state directory and title. Baseline and AV1 state/factories remain distinct. Candidate capability/preferences presentation is narrowed to CPU H.265, slow, quality 25, no recipe or vendor profile; Remux and production-qualified catalog status are explicitly unavailable. The title/status warns that this is an experimental range route, cancellation requires relaunch and native threading is unqualified. These are implemented source routes, not observed native startup.

### 4. Reuse output authority without calling a type check an unforgeable brand

The candidate factory now accepts a main-owned `OutputAccess` object and passes it through the ordinary service integration. Both the helper and factory reject callback-shaped substitutes and require `instanceof OutputAccess` when the object is supplied. That is a nominal type check, not an unforgeable brand and not independent authorization to open any path.

Actual output actions continue to depend on the existing class's private grant and identity machinery. No renderer-supplied callbacks, arbitrary paths or new IPC permission are introduced. The existing output-access tests are part of the unchanged 125-check baseline and final affected suite, while the new seam test proves rejection/acceptance and pass-through under injection. It does not show an actual newly encoded output receiving a live open/reveal capability through this workbench. That integration still belongs in the native journey.

### 5. Keep per-file editing useful and cancellation visibly terminal

The service test exercises actual `OperationDraft` edits, `updateFileOperations` reprepare lifecycle, final encode-plan construction and the real proportional family selector. The explicit range is 130–132 seconds with Keep source and precision FLAC. The test verifies a new plan ID, a ready range entry and removal of unqualified compatibility authority before update emission. Runtime, raw-report evidence, inspection worker and runtime token are injected. A genuine selector object in this controlled fixture is not an observed live runtime grant.

The renderer tests execute the complete production renderer in a small DOM/event fixture using the original HTML. An unsupported inspection-only row cannot be selected or started but can be edited through the existing per-file range/audio controls and existing `updateFileOperations` API. The fixture sends 0.613–2.721 seconds with precision FLAC; no batch-range API is added. The test covers data flow and attributes, not native layout, screen-reader output, system dialogs or actual encoding.

Candidate cancellation emits closure status after synchronously closing service admission. The renderer retains a sticky closed flag: later higher-revision snapshots cannot reopen new work merely by omitting the terminal state or presenting a ready row. It disables preparation, selection/Start and editing after closure, while candidate mode disables new native qualification and Remux preparation. The existing close/relaunch instruction remains visible instead of promising same-session retry. These renderer changes are conditional on candidate metadata; the ordinary interface is not replaced.

A separate VM test executes main's lifecycle with a delayed startup service. A close request arrives before service resolution; once the owner arrives, closure waits for resource drainage and diagnostics before exit, without constructing a window or registering IPC. This is useful source-level ordering evidence, not an actual Electron close-during-startup observation or an OS child-drain measurement.

### 6. Preserve failures, review corrections and final source evidence

The first new renderer fixture run, `20261117T162754Z_candidate-workbench-renderer-first`, passed one of two tests. It used the nonexistent draft method `registerFiles`; correcting the fixture to `addSources` produced the retained 127/127 result at `20261117T162817Z_candidate-workbench-renderer-fixed`, including the unchanged 125 parent checks. This was a test-fixture mistake, not an observed production failure, and the red result was not discarded.

Subsequent focused lifecycle/service checks included 19/19 at `20261117T163109Z_candidate-workbench-transient-review` and five of five at `20261117T163212Z_candidate-workbench-startup-drain`. The frozen worker source result and independent v1 review each recorded 158/158 with no source blocker. Those totals describe their own selected suites, not a substitute for the final lead run or a whole-platform pass.

The lead then found that test descriptions claimed more than their bodies established. The “pending write” window is modeled by an emitter/IIFE around a manually controlled promise. It does not execute the actual base `prepareItem` → `recordIncident` awaited production write. The disconnected old-item snippet is not a real delayed prepare callback, and the runtime token in the range-reprepare fixture is injected. V2 corrects the titles/comments and handoff to say so; runtime implementation code did not change between those reviewed versions. The reviewer agreed that this exposed coverage limits rather than a demonstrated source blocker. Actual base-await traversal, overlapping late preparation and live output-capability issuance remain future coverage gaps.

The final nine-file v2 patch is SHA-256 `36529e8708fd0145ba1e318fa9f1f6efda1e4604d792e21c6a3e696af9f4dd06`. It covers the runtime-choice module, workbench main, candidate service and test, AV1 routing test, shared renderer, new candidate-workbench helper/test and new candidate renderer test. No shared `EncoderService`, contract, IPC, preload, HTML, theme or Windows main file was changed. The existing AV1 source-match assertion was adapted to the intentional three-way routing, not removed to hide a missing old runtime.

Lead receipt `20261117T163621Z_candidate-workbench-lead-final` passed 163/163, exit zero. The separate `20261117T163622Z_candidate-workbench-lead-linux` broad run had 181 passes and 27 failures out of 208, exit one. Its normalized failure diagnostics match the prior broad run; the 28 raw diagnostic lines include wrapper entries and are not 28 failed tests. Ten additional passes and the same inherited 27 failures do not make the broad suite green or prove blocked branches. Both final receipts record no source-byte changes during their runs and identify parent `dfa1658`; the changed integration was not yet committed.

### 7. Keep native availability and preservation status separate from source readiness

Read-only inspection of the selected workspace found no installed Electron in the checked project/PATH/common locations and no active display/Xvfb environment. A package declaration or an Xorg executable is not a running supported Electron/display session. This is a result about the environment inspected, not proof that every connected machine or future environment lacks those facilities. No Electron, GUI, native media runtime, browser process or installation was launched for this source step.

The warning diagnosis from checkpoint 009 remains unchanged. Duplicate codec-option precedence was directly explained; missing sysfs/NUMA topology remains a strongly supported, qualified mechanism for x265 pool warnings, not a sole-cause experiment. Native threading/performance is still unqualified. No stderr suppression, new pool flags, sysfs mount, affinity/security change or runtime repin was introduced to make the workbench appear ready.

The new [execution record](2026-11-17_Candidate_Workbench_Integration.md) and [receipt](2026-11-17_Candidate_Workbench_Integration_Receipts.json) own the scoped source/test result, alongside the lead-owned [CURRENT_STATE](CURRENT_STATE.md). This entry preserves the previous 255,403-byte summary as an exact suffix. Historical archives and the earlier correction receipt remain untouched. At this record's cutoff, checkpoint 010's commit, Notion restore/readback and GitHub publication are still pending; checkpoint 009's verified destinations do not back up this new patch automatically.

## 🧠 Key Insights

### Editable does not need to mean qualified or startable

The existing per-file editor provides a smaller route than changing batch semantics or inventing a new pre-prepare form. Retaining a draft while removing runnable compatibility authority lets the user state the missing range explicitly. The distinction depends on the service guard and snapshot/plan treatment, not on disabling a button alone. The source tests support that separation, while native preparation and real awaited timing still need their own evidence.

### Source reuse can preserve product boundaries better than a new shell

The candidate path now uses the existing main, renderer, preload, IPC and theme architecture rather than a parallel application. Its exact option, isolated state and conditional metadata keep the experiment visible without changing default factories. Reuse does not by itself prove end-to-end behavior: the new acquisition path lacks restored tools, and a native application has not been opened.

### A passing test's name is part of the evidence contract

The v2 correction matters even though it changed no runtime code. A controlled pending promise or disconnected object can establish a modeled boundary but cannot be promoted to an executed base-class write or delayed callback. Describing the fixture precisely makes the remaining test work actionable instead of presenting a misleading green check. The same discipline applies to `instanceof`: it is useful main-owned type validation, not the private authority mechanism itself.

### Native qualification needs both the right environment and the right inputs

Finding an Electron binary alone would not restore the exact candidate tools, dependencies and original raw report. Restoring those files alone would not supply a supported display or prove GUI lifecycle and output opening. The next proof must establish each prerequisite explicitly and retain the warning/performance caveat, rather than weakening a gate because the source integration is already tested.

## 📌 Decisions Made

- **Confirmed by the lead:** Add only the exact `--blue-runtime=9.0.2-keep720-candidate` developer route; preserve baseline/AV1 defaults, factories and state isolation. No production activation is implied.
- **Confirmed design choice:** Reuse unsupported-but-editable draft rows and existing per-file range controls. Do not add range to batch defaults, invent a range, qualify Full or modify shared contract/IPC semantics.
- **Confirmed authority boundary:** Remove candidate compatibility-ready plan entries before snapshot emission and retain the original Start guard; use main-owned fixed runtime acquisition and the existing private output-grant/identity machinery.
- **Confirmed lifecycle:** Cancellation stays closed to new work and visibly requires close/relaunch. No hidden candidate renewal or same-service recovery is added.
- **Confirmed review correction:** Keep the initial renderer-fixture failure; correct v2 test descriptions to identify modeled pending-write/stale-object behavior and injected runtime evidence. Do not claim that actual base-await/overlap coverage was obtained.
- **Confirmed next direction, not yet executed:** Preserve the source checkpoint, locate a supported native Electron/display environment separately, restore exact reviewed runtime inputs only through bounded setup, then independently review and run the existing-UI journey. Continue independent remaining 0.7 work if native proof stays blocked.
- **Deferred:** Default/catalog activation, arbitrary runtime paths, broader geometry/audio, same-service recovery, native performance/GPU/Windows qualification, release acceptance and packaging. Ahmed retains acceptance; packaging remains last.

## ⚠️ Difficulties / Friction

The principal design tension was the initial Full-shaped inspection path. Simply adding a candidate branch would leave a UI that needs a draft before showing range controls, while the service does not qualify Full. Broadening batch defaults or marking the compatibility plan ready would solve the presentation problem by weakening the operation contract. The chosen implementation instead preserves editing and removes runnable authority; the actual awaited-preparation and overlap cases remain explicitly unproven.

Main integration also had genuine shape mismatches: output access was previously rejected by the strict candidate options and main expected a Remux status field. The new helper/factory seam resolves those specific interfaces without expanding renderer authority or claiming Remux qualification. A nominal `instanceof` check must not be oversold as the security boundary; private grants and identity checks still decide actions.

The first renderer test failed because its fixture used the wrong draft method. Later review found misleading descriptions around controlled timing fixtures. Both issues were corrected and retained, but they are different: one was an executable fixture error; the other was an evidence-description overclaim with remaining coverage work. Neither should be described as a native app failure or as proof that the untested production paths are safe.

Native validation is currently blocked by the inspected environment and missing fixed-path inputs, not by a failing real UI campaign. No such campaign ran. The pre-existing 27 broad Linux failures and the runtime topology caveat remain, so a focused green source suite cannot justify “the app is usable now” or “all Linux tests pass.”

## 🔧 Adjustments Made

- Shifted from: A calibration-backed service proven through an external bounded launcher but not wired to the existing visible workbench.
  - To: An explicit developer-only main/renderer route with fixed acquisition, narrow capability/status presentation and unchanged default routes.
  - Because: The preceding actual service proof supported a finite consumer integration, while owner direction favored reusing existing design and architecture.
  - Consequence: Source integration can be checkpointed; native usability remains a separate experiment rather than a claimed consequence.
- Shifted from: An existing draft-before-range UI whose initial compatibility/Full-shaped row was not qualified by the candidate.
  - To: Existing editable inspection-only rows, candidate-only snapshot demotion/entry removal and explicit per-file reprepare.
  - Because: Range is excluded from batch defaults and unsupported draft rows already expose the necessary editor.
  - Consequence: No shared contract expansion, invented range or Full qualification was needed; the original Start guard remains authoritative.
- Shifted from: Test wording suggesting actual awaited base preparation and delayed callbacks.
  - To: Exact descriptions of a modeled pending-write window, disconnected old-item fixture and injected runtime/report/token evidence.
  - Because: Lead review compared the descriptions to the test bodies rather than relying on a green total.
  - Consequence: V2 changes comments/titles, not runtime behavior, and explicitly preserves the missing real-await/overlap/live-output coverage for continuation.

## ❌ Mistakes / Risks Noted

- **Actual fixture mistake, corrected:** `registerFiles` was not the draft API; the retained first renderer run passed one of two. The fixture now uses `addSources`, and the subsequent selected suite passed 127/127. No red record was erased.
- **Actual evidence-description overclaim, corrected:** Modeled emission around a pending promise and a disconnected old-item assignment were described too strongly. V2 narrows the claims. ## 🎯 Session Goal

Checkpoint 008 had delivered a reviewed composition and lifecycle fixes, but its positive construction tests used injected dependencies. The new runtime had already passed short and long calibration, yet calibration alone could not establish normal service preparation, Start, storage, output publication or live cancellation. The purpose of this session was to cross that specific gap without expanding into an application rollout.

The initial intended stopping point was one retained, reviewed result against unchanged source. The final audit exposed successful-encode diagnostics that warranted a subsequent read-only cause investigation; that investigation was added after the actual run, not presented as an already-resolved starting assumption. The planned five cases were a late two-second trim from the existing 150-second fixture, a nonaligned range from a newly generated independent eight-second source, an output appearing after preview, cancellation during an actual live encode, and a successful operation only after closing the old owners and opening new ones. A failed attempt or missed cancellation observation was to remain evidence, not trigger an undisclosed retry.

The session aimed to:

- Exercise the real developer-only EncoderService composition with its genuine candidate handle, exact original calibration report and immutable producer gates.
- Verify selected frame/sample accounting, FLAC PCM identity, output metadata and decode checks, proportional limits and normal evidence-storage records for successful outputs.
- Observe collision protection and active-encode cancellation without overwriting a sentinel, publishing a cancelled output or reusing a revoked owner.
- Retain the complete result, audit saved evidence independently and keep claims no broader than this Linux CPU 720p30 experiment.

## 🛠️ Actions Taken

### 1. Preserve checkpoint 008 and distinguish its publication chronology from execution

Checkpoint 008 is local `374d327a7e3fa934d046def5b4e18b45285680ed`, with an exact 514-file source snapshot and complete eight-commit local-history bundle. The [Notion checkpoint](https://app.notion.com/p/41bbd7ade63b81f8bfd9e35605405e32) was restored and verified. Its [full native session page](https://app.notion.com/p/41bbd7ade63b81449deec038fa2d56bf) was fetched unabridged: 212,471 source bytes, SHA-256 `9e5a55e36a624586c11248300d9d2bb873dedda69fc18e2c20019ec5fab90451`.

The 16:05:31 UTC launch gate required that local commit, verified Notion restore, the exact independently reviewed launcher and frozen source hashes. It did not claim that GitHub verification was already complete. Checkpoint 008's GitHub preservation was subsequently verified at 16:07:33 UTC, after this campaign had finished, as `14be3e1e218d2ea97cef10583b8d166ebd771e96` on `CipherVaults/FFmpeg-Encoder-Blue-Linux`, branch `blue/linux-development`, sole parent `74a82817b57ad929be56e9137e4e7dda3086097d`.

The remote tree contained 896 files: 889 unchanged, three modified and four new, with no deletions. The seven changed contents, parent, tree and final ref were checked without force. This completed preservation belongs to checkpoint 008's source and records; it is not publication of the new campaign evidence described here.

### 2. Approve one bounded launcher against the genuine composition

The external launcher remained SHA-256 `b28cddc0f9f738f46e8716fa1292207a35f3526c055dec84fd0025227ec42ca5`. It used a one-attempt marker, the genuine candidate service factory and candidate runtime pair `1980b0c0480dba85e00fb69f6994e9fd022b46d6355538ee24a445c87da337b9`. The factory consumed the exact original long-calibration report, SHA-256 `1feb0f2958c78cc73683c70fed08835d1e61b98462e8b30e0127f95b5cf350ab`, not its portable archival derivative. The reviewed 97-file calibration-producer closure and seven listed runtime dependencies remained prerequisites; the launcher additionally gated 271 source records.

Independent launch review identified the need to distinguish the launcher's post-close runtime/artifact checks from a full producer-source recheck. The lead therefore retained the all-514-file freeze through the later audit and supplied its own full-tree verification. The review also retained the rule that a too-fast encode which escaped active-cancellation observation would be a failure, not an invitation to repeat until it passed.

The campaign ceiling was 180 children at concurrency one, a 30-minute abort request, at least 27 GiB free before child admission and a two-GiB owned-artifact admission allowance. Existing per-operation proportional resources were used unchanged. These are admission and observation controls, not a hard active-write disk ceiling or a guaranteed whole-process exit deadline. The main app, default factory, production pins and catalog were not activated or replaced.

### 3. Run the late-source and independent-source publications through the real service

The launcher copied the existing 150-second fixture under an owned different filename and verified its full hash as `b2adc00b3f7e4a2f459023c9dfe484451b8eedfdd3c5114542abc413b3ca893b`. This remained the calibration source by content; renaming it did not make it independent. It separately generated a new eight-second vertically flipped source with distinct stereo chirps, full hash `882bbbd151ea501205a57f8cae33416cbddbb2757c02efffe4fa73b6753091cb`, and verified that this hash did not occur among the calibration sources. This new fixture is not the previous campaign's eleven-second held-out source.

The first normal-service operation selected 130–132 seconds of the long source. The service prepared a ready plan, obtained a genuine proportional selection, started the encode, collected operation evidence and published the local output. The result had 60 selected/output frames and 96,000 selected/output samples. The selected source and output PCM hashes matched, with 384,000 bytes each. The complete source accounting covered 4,500 frames and 7,200,000 samples rather than treating the two-second range as the whole source.

The next operation used 0.613–2.721 seconds of the new eight-second source. It published 63 selected/output frames and 101,184 selected/output samples, with matching source/output PCM hashes and 404,736 bytes each. The full eight-second source accounting covered 240 frames and 384,000 samples. Non-frame-aligned requested duration was not incorrectly rounded to an assumed integer number of frames.

For each successful publication, the launcher checked generated-output metadata, HEVC video plus FLAC audio, 1280×720 geometry, 1:1 sample aspect ratio, 16:9 display aspect ratio and 1/1000 stream time bases, then ran the output through FFmpeg's error-sensitive full-decode check. These were actual producer checks within the one campaign. The later auditor did not rerun them. Normal evidence storage reported persisted completed records, no recorded final violations and released reservations; its bounded point observations still do not constitute a hard write cap.

### 4. Protect an output appearing after preview

The collision case prepared a range from the fresh source, then wrote an owned sentinel at the intended destination before Start. The service returned `skipped` with “Output appeared since preview.” The sentinel was byte-identical after the operation and retained SHA-256 `541e7f3289bcf1fd9b36608330013a775380c53fbf579763a9c3e0445b8368b2`.

The count of progress-bearing children stayed at 14 before and after this case. This supports the intended no-output-encode collision outcome in this sequence, together with the reviewed Start path and retained sentinel; it is not a blanket claim about every filesystem race or all overwrite policies. No collision was cleared or silently worked around to obtain a passing encode.

### 5. Observe active encoding, cancel, drain and close before fresh-instance recovery

The cancellation case requested 0–7 seconds of the fresh source. Cancellation was triggered only after the service reported `stage: encoding`, a live child, frame 8, 0.490667 seconds of progress and `ended: false`. The item then became `cancelled`, the final destination did not exist, admission was closed and a further Start on that same service was rejected with `SHUTTING_DOWN`.

The incomplete temporary output was deliberately retained because it had never been identity-bound. “No published output” must not be rewritten as “no file left behind” or “all temporary media deleted.” Its record preserves that safety outcome and the diagnostic reason. The saved cancellation details retain 1,086 bytes of stderr: duplicate-codec warnings, x265 CPU-mask/thread-pool diagnostics and interrupted demux/PCM errors. The final audit also found the codec/CPU-mask/thread-pool diagnostics on all three successful target encodes, so they cannot be attributed solely to cancellation. Their presence is not hidden by the external launcher's empty stderr.

The captured sequence then drained the operation, closed the old service/runtime owners and recorded that closure before opening a fresh candidate handle and a fresh service. The new instance published a separate 1.013–1.513-second range from the independent eight-second source: 15 selected/output frames, 24,000 selected/output samples and matching PCM hashes with 96,000 bytes each. The two closure records both had admission closed, `busy: false` and no reported closure errors.

This is measured fresh-instance recovery after active-encode cancellation. It does not prove same-service retry, idle-cancellation token revocation, cancellation during evidence collection or general crash recovery. No revoked token was revived to make the recovery case work.

### 6. Reconcile campaign accounting, source freeze and the independent audit

The one actual attempt finished successfully with five cases, three local output publications, 119 children spawned and closed, zero live and maximum concurrency one. The retained owned inventory measured 88,343,227 bytes before the final report was written. The report's internal elapsed value was 50,704 ms; the enclosing launcher exited zero after 51.159 seconds of wall time. No retry occurred.

The raw report is `candidate-normal-service-proof-20261117/owned-proof-LGmb6T/report.json`, 85,306 bytes, SHA-256 `0f82cfbb028b2da5cc7c036af69096ea87099b063c5aa235ea39763a0d42e70d`. Its `census.encodes: 28` counts all children whose arguments contain `-progress`, including inspection/decoding. It must not be read as 28 output encodes or 28 publications. There were three completed publications and one observed cancelled target encode.

The worker rehashed all 271 gated source records at 16:07:09 UTC with no mismatch. The lead independently checked every one of the 514 tracked files against checkpoint 008 at 16:08:24 UTC, with zero changes and the source/document freeze still held. The latest source-test results remained the preceding 97/97 affected checks and broad 171 passes/27 inherited failures out of 198; those suites were not rerun or relabelled green for this evidence-only step.

The final independent saved-output audit, initially dated 16:08:57 UTC and subsequently extended with child-diagnostic analysis, is 111,726 bytes, SHA-256 `45fb2d5d2de9c350e72b2464a8021d7c7a251cb889655d938c94c32684e5573c`; it supersedes the earlier audit digests. It checked all 271 gated producers, all 171 retained inventory artifacts, both source contents and the copied source's original, nine runtime/dependency files, all three output hashes/receipts, collision preservation, cancellation/closure order and the recorded child census. It independently rederived all three issued resource limits and checked all six telemetry-stream summaries against their issued byte/record limits. It found the saved evidence supports the stated narrow Linux CPU 720p30 precision-FLAC normal-service proof, collision and fresh-instance recovery, not product activation.

The audit did not independently re-decode media, retain every raw streamed child byte, independently recompute sparse digests/birthtimes, supply atomic loader guarantees or cryptographically attest chronology. Full source hashes and saved/current identity fields were checked; those limits remain explicit. The external launcher's stderr was empty. Each of the three successful target encodes retained 499 stderr bytes containing duplicate-codec warnings, x265 “Failed to genrate CPU mask” and thread-pool warnings; the cancelled target retained 1,086 bytes including those plus interrupted demux/PCM errors. All 21 collector children separately recorded empty stderr, zero bytes and `truncated: false`. The 499 bytes in each job's accounting belong to its target encode, not the collector or progress text. At the audit cutoff, root cause, performance and broader reliability implications remained unresolved; the later read-only investigation below explains option precedence and supports a qualified topology mechanism without proving native behavior; the narrow output/PCM/timing/publication assertions passed, but “all child stderr clean” would be false.

### 7. Diagnose retained warnings read-only without hiding them or repeating the campaign

After the final audit clarified that all three successful targets carried diagnostics, a separate read-only investigation inspected the saved actual argument lists, retained verified FFmpeg 9.0.2 source, static x265/libnuma archive symbols and strings, current workspace topology/affinity and official upstream source/documentation. No FFmpeg/x265 or library function was executed, no media was rerun and no runtime, system setting or application source was changed. The lead read the resulting `warning-diagnosis/DIAGNOSIS.md`, SHA-256 `258df9a07f49c4163cea1b1071fc376a5347ad6c77e8e3fe8bb34c3cbbba0ce7`.

The duplicate-codec warning has a directly established cause. Saved actual arguments place the application's global `-c copy` before specific HEVC and FLAC overrides. The retained exact FFmpeg option-matcher source warns about multiple matching options but uses the final matching option. That deterministic precedence and the actual HEVC/FLAC output evidence explain the warning; they do not justify blindly removing global copy, which still governs streams intended to be copied. Any later argument canonicalization needs its own reviewed tests and fingerprint/producer-gate consequences.

The CPU-mask/thread-pool result has a strong but qualified mechanism. The owned x265 4.1 static archive references `numa_node_to_cpus`. The same workspace's post-run observation found no `/sys`, while process affinity still exposed CPUs 0–8; absent topology does not mean there were no CPUs. Matching libnuma 2.0.19 source reads the sysfs node CPU-map file and fails when it is unavailable. Accessible x265 upstream master places the exact misspelled diagnostic on that call's failure and disables pool-dependent WPP/lookahead features when no pool was allocated. These sources and archive evidence strongly support missing topology as the mechanism, not simply an unavailable encoder.

The exact x265 4.1 source was not retrieved, there was no trace of the completed child and topology was observed after the run rather than captured inside it. The result is therefore “strongly consistent with missing sysfs/NUMA topology,” not “experimentally proven sole cause.” Native threading, throughput and broader reliability remain unqualified. The additional cancelled-child demux/PCM diagnostics are consistent with interruption given the live cancellation, unchanged source hashes and later fresh-instance success; the precise packet state was not independently traced.

The lead accepted keeping the measured functional proof within those limits and proceeding to separately reviewed developer-only UI wiring with an explicit caveat. No stderr suppression, forced pool flags, sysfs mount, affinity/security change or runtime repin was made. A native-host proof remains a separate environment and bounded-execution gate. The read-only finding changes the next step from unexamined warnings to qualified integration planning, not to an unqualified runtime-readiness claim.

### 8. Record the result without turning portable evidence into live authority

The new [execution narrative](2026-11-17_Candidate_Normal_Service_Proof.md), [compact evidence receipt](2026-11-17_Candidate_Normal_Service_Proof_Receipts.json) and [portable full report](evidence-2026-11-17/normal-service-proof-portable.json) have been saved locally as the lead-owned record set for this result. The 89,603-byte portable report, SHA-256 `a3a2b8bed23166fc0a56734ac0034bcde92c4c1b7f94a21b6b41ae5a3b070582`, preserves the full report while transforming 26 path-bearing strings to explicit logical prefixes. Its metadata identifies the raw original hash and its non-raw, non-authoritative status; without the excluded mapping it cannot reconstruct the original absolute-path bytes. It cannot replace the exact raw long-calibration input required by the service factory.

Raw runtime binaries, source/output media, retained incomplete cancellation artifact and original raw report remain local-only. Hashes and portable records preserve identification and explain the experiment, not the bytes needed to reproduce it on a different machine. A source/history mirror must not be described as a full media/runtime backup.

This new entry preserves the prior 212,471-byte cumulative summary as an exact suffix. Checkpoints 001–008, the original historical archive and the earlier correction receipt remain untouched. At this writing, the new evidence checkpoint's commit, verified Notion restoration and GitHub ref are still to be completed and verified by their owners; the already completed checkpoint 008 destinations do not establish those future outcomes.

## 🧠 Key Insights

### A real service result closes a different gap from calibration or injected construction

The new evidence shows that the exact reviewed developer-only composition can use a genuine runtime and original calibration to reach actual prepare/Start, evidence collection and saved output. That was missing from checkpoint 008's controlled positive tests. It does not broaden the qualifying family: the successful sources are generated Linux CPU 720p30 examples, and the longest requested interval among the completed outputs is only 2.108 seconds. The 150-second input tests late selection and whole-source accounting, not a long sustained normal-service output.

### Refusal and retention can be the successful result

The collision case passed by not overwriting an existing destination. Cancellation passed by ending the active job, withholding publication, refusing another Start on the closing owner and retaining an unbound incomplete artifact instead of deleting what it could not safely identify. Treating every passed case as an output file or every retained file as cleanup failure would erase the contracts this campaign was designed to test.

### Metrics need their producer's definition

The misleadingly named `encodes` counter is a concrete example: its value 28 measures progress-bearing children, not output jobs. Likewise 50,704 ms and 51.159 seconds measure different scopes, and empty launcher stderr says nothing about collected child stderr. The durable lesson here is to retain the observable label and explain its meaning rather than silently relabel raw fields or inflate a summary from their names.

### Independent audit strengthens traceability without becoming a second experiment

Hashing retained evidence and rederiving resource limits provide useful independent checks on the producer's report. They do not reproduce PCM decoding, prove general visual fidelity or recover unretained raw streams. The audit's value is strongest when its verified claims and its non-executed checks are both preserved.

## 📌 Decisions Made

- **Confirmed by the lead:** Permit exactly one reviewed bounded normal-service campaign against immutable checkpoint 008 after local Git and verified Notion restoration; preserve failure rather than automatically retry.
- **Confirmed execution choice:** Use the genuine developer-only factory and exact original calibration/runtime gates, not VM test substitutes, portable report authority or production pin changes.
- **Confirmed lifecycle boundary:** Active cancellation closes admission; drain and close the old owners before creating both a new handle and a new service. No same-service recovery claim is added.
- **Confirmed evidence treatment:** Preserve the collision sentinel, the cancelled incomplete artifact and its warnings/errors. Do not turn empty outer stderr into a child-cleanliness claim.
- **Confirmed documentation correction:** Explain `encodes: 28` as progress-bearing children while leaving the raw report unchanged; preserve all prior session bytes and separate completed checkpoint 008 publication from the new checkpoint still pending.
- **Completed subsequent read-only step:** Diagnose warnings without suppressing criteria or re-encoding. Duplicate-codec precedence is directly explained; missing sysfs/NUMA topology is a strongly supported but not sole-cause-proven mechanism. Native threading/performance remains unqualified.
- **Confirmed next direction by the lead, not yet performed:** Separately review finite developer-workbench integration that reuses the existing shell/theme, requires explicit candidate choice, changes no defaults and makes cancellation require close/relaunch. Inspect the existing range-before-Full access risk before implementation.
- **Deferred:** Production/app/default/catalog activation, broader geometry, long normal-service outputs, evidence-collection cancellation, native/GPU/Windows qualification and owner acceptance. This campaign does not authorize those outcomes or an immediate native/media run.

## ⚠️ Difficulties / Friction

The main difficulty was proof scope, not an encode failure. A short successful output from a long source can be mistaken for long-output qualification; an independently generated fixture can be confused with an independent auditor; a passing cancellation can be mistaken for same-service recovery. The concrete sources, ranges, instance boundary and review role are therefore part of the result, not optional footnotes.

Resource safety also has two layers. The launcher's 27-GiB/two-GiB admission checks and the service's normal evidence-storage observations reduce risk at known points, but neither promises to stop every active write at a byte ceiling. The 30-minute mechanism requests abort; it does not guarantee the entire process has exited then. No bound was enlarged to obtain this successful run.

Retained subprocess diagnostics limit a “clean run” description. All three successful target encodes had the same classes of duplicate-codec and CPU-mask/thread-pool diagnostics; only the cancelled target additionally had interrupted demux/PCM errors. The collectors themselves had empty stderr. The bounded assertions and saved-output audit passed, but those facts do not establish the diagnostics' root cause, performance impact or general harmlessness. Their presence on successful targets made read-only diagnosis necessary rather than allowing dismissal as cancellation noise. That subsequent investigation established codec-option precedence and a qualified topology mechanism, but did not remove native-performance or broader-reliability uncertainty.

During audit, an initial read used the nonexistent filename `encoder-telemetry.json`; directory inspection corrected the reference to the actual retained `encoder-input-receipt.json`. This was read-only review friction with no source/media effect, not a missing-evidence failure or a reason to manufacture a replacement.

## 🧾 Session Outcome

The reviewed candidate composition has moved from controlled construction tests to one actual normal-service campaign on immutable checkpoint 008. The five cases passed without retries: three bounded local publications with frame/sample and selected PCM evidence, a preserved post-preview collision sentinel, and observed active-encode cancellation followed by new-instance recovery. A subsequent read-only investigation explained duplicate-codec precedence and supported a qualified missing-topology mechanism for x265 pool failure; it did not alter source/runtime/settings or qualify native behavior. The raw diagnostics, cancelled retained artifact, local-only data boundary and unchanged inherited test failures remain visible.

The outcome is a narrow developer-only Linux CPU 720p30 precision-FLAC service proof, not a production activation, long-output qualification, same-service recovery or cross-platform result. The next checkpoint's preservation must still be completed and verified; only checkpoint 008's publication is reported as done here.

**Five-case functional result: High within the stated scope.**

The experiment used generated fixtures, short published ranges and one Linux CPU family. Same-service recovery, evidence-collection cancellation, long output, general geometry/quality, GUI/native/GPU/Windows behavior and production readiness remain outside this confidence claim.

## 🎯 Session Goal

The short and long calibration campaigns answered whether the selected new runtime could satisfy a narrow generated-media contract. They did not prove that normal application service preparation, Start, storage, publication or cancellation worked with that candidate. The intended next step was therefore not another calibration or a production pin update. It was a developer-only composition that could exercise the existing real service under explicit evidence and runtime gates.

The initial goal was to make that composition reviewable and testable while keeping old factories, defaults and catalog behavior unchanged. Independent review later exposed two concrete lifecycle problems in the first version: admission closed too late during cancellation, and one cleanup error could hide another. Those corrections became requirements during the work; they were not retroactively presented as discoveries already resolved at its start.

The session aimed to:

- Admit only a genuine candidate handle with the exact reviewed runtime/dependency identity and original long-calibration report.
- Bind that report to its immutable producer closure and reuse the real service/selector/evidence-storage contracts.
- Preserve cancellation invalidation and require fresh-instance recovery rather than silently reviving a stale token.
- Finish a reviewed, tested source checkpoint before any actual normal-service experiment.

## 🛠️ Actions Taken

### 1. Establish what the preceding checkpoint actually preserved

Checkpoint 007 is local `f352cac682f52f01c9ffddf076d9445d178d8da0`. Its [Notion checkpoint](https://app.notion.com/p/41bbd7ade63b8138a2a6ddb682306d0c) restored all 510 source files exactly and cloned the complete seven-commit bundle. The [full corrected session page](https://app.notion.com/p/41bbd7ade63b817e8fafd1dc1f7c5271) was read back unabridged, with all seven prior sessions and the final ending present. The earlier summary-correction request therefore reached a verified readable and source-restorable destination, not merely a local draft.

GitHub publication was independently verified as `74a82817b57ad929be56e9137e4e7dda3086097d`, sole parent `11bce729aed4a261c87e6cdd213f29c1f9efe2d0`, on the existing private Linux branch. Seven changed files were checked, 885 remained unchanged and none were deleted. The preservation cycle completed at 15:45:27 UTC. This result belongs to checkpoint 007. None of the new service code described below is thereby committed or backed up.

### 2. Gate a developer-only service against exact original evidence

The patch adds `candidate-keep720-service.cjs` and its test, and extracts a runtime-parameterized receipt-validation helper from `workbench-long-range-service.cjs`. The historical wrapper continues to use its original pinned behavior. The new composition is main-owned and not imported into the app, renderer, CLI or ordinary production factory route.

Admission requires a genuine candidate runtime handle for pair identity `1980b0c0480dba85e00fb69f6994e9fd022b46d6355538ee24a445c87da337b9` and the exact seven inspected dependency records. The factory rejects loader overrides, checks runtime currentness around awaited work, and verifies the 97-file literal-relative CommonJS producer closure against immutable `54ee143`. This closure describes the old calibration producers, not the newly authored composition itself; the new files have their own reviewed patch identity.

The factory reads only the original 55,953-byte long-calibration report at its fixed canonical, non-symlink path. It opens an ordinary single-link file, bounds positioned reads, compares before/after identity stamps and requires SHA-256 `1feb0f2958c78cc73683c70fed08835d1e61b98462e8b30e0127f95b5cf350ab`. The transformed portable archival JSON is deliberately not a substitute. A pure caller-selected digest seam supports tests; the live factory still uses the fixed original digest and path.

That distinction preserves the project’s earlier evidence decision: a portable record can explain and preserve the result without impersonating the raw artifact required to grant a live operation. Missing the original report remains a real prerequisite failure, not a reason to accept a similar-looking JSON object.

### 3. Reuse the normal service without opening production scope

The new factory composes the existing candidate trim service with a current runtime observer, clock-qualified family selector, genuine proportional resources, telemetry and normal evidence storage. The selected family remains CPU HEVC slow/quality 25, original 720p geometry and precision-FLAC range output. Its qualification and timing records come from the reviewed long report; telemetry validity and the exact two reported input streams are checked rather than assumed.

Tool execution stays behind the genuine candidate runner with repeated loader/currentness checks. Inspection is single-worker and vendor capability discovery remains explicitly incomplete instead of implying GPU support. There is no catalog/default change, historical report/pin replacement, broader resize family or automatic application activation.

Positive factory construction in the tests is VM-injected. It proves the composition logic under controlled dependencies, not that the fixed original report, genuine runtime and normal service have already been opened successfully together in a live operation. The next real campaign remains necessary.

### 4. Reproduce and fix the cancellation-admission race

In v1, the cancellation path could await superclass work before closing new admission. A pending preference write or terminal callback can yield; keeping admission open during that interval leaves a timing window for another action. Independent review required the property to hold at the synchronous call boundary, not only after `cancelCurrent()` eventually resolves.

The regression was retained in the 15:50:55 UTC red run, which had eight passes and two intended failures across the cancellation/cleanup controls. V2 calls `closeAdmission()` before returning or awaiting the superclass cancellation work. The final tests specifically cover pending saved-write drainage, so the fix is more than moving a comment or relying on the ordinary fast path.

The resulting lifecycle is explicit: request cancellation, drain the operation, close resources, then open a fresh candidate handle **and** a fresh service for a recovery attempt. Idle cancellation does not by itself always revoke an externally held candidate token; closing this service’s admission and later resource closure are distinct actions. The code must not claim same-service recovery or revive the old handle merely to reuse a convenient smoke test.

### 5. Preserve both resource-closure failures

The first closure sequence could mask one failure with another. V2 attempts the base service cleanup and candidate close independently. If one fails, it rethrows that original error. If both fail, it raises an `AggregateError` containing the base failure first and the candidate failure second.

This preserves diagnostic meaning without turning a failed close into success. It also avoids an unsafe inference: reaching the second cleanup attempt does not prove that the first resource actually closed. Tests cover both failure ordering and the single-error identity, while actual OS-child drainage and media cancellation remain future measured evidence.

### 6. Reconcile final tests, unchanged failures and the source handoff

The final v2 patch is SHA-256 `d65a081ad1f6ba4f66896171cdb4e5e177c05bc3097703a2aa772709750451c7`. Only three code/test paths are included. The final worker green run at 15:51:07 UTC passed 35/35; an independent final repeat also passed 35/35 with no blocking review finding. The earlier 33/33 result belongs to v1, not the corrected final source.

Lead receipt `20261117T155240Z_candidate-service-lead-final` passed 97/97. The separate broad Linux receipt `20261117T155242Z_candidate-service-lead-linux` had 171 passes and 27 failures out of 198. Its normalized failure-diagnostic set exactly matches the preceding 161-pass/27-failure run; wrapper diagnostic entries are not extra failed tests. Ten additional passing tests and an unchanged failure-name set do not make the broad suite green or prove masked branches.

The legacy comparison was independently repeated on immutable parent `f352cac` and the changed tree: both passed four of five, with the same missing historical long-range report `ENOENT`. That is inherited-failure parity, not a resolved report dependency or a claim that the blocked branch succeeded. The worker’s early legacy result and its cause remain retained.

The lead also reran the historical re-entry and pre-stage phase checks. They did not pass wholesale: the old Windows-branch/local-remote and unavailable-baseline assumptions do not describe this reconstruction, and a pre-existing `.codex/hooks.json` toolkit drift remained. The re-entry check did confirm an empty index, no Planning stage and the 43-row requirement inventory. No hook repair, rebaseline or claim that those historical scripts were green was made; the actual reconstruction manifest and explicit staged-scope checks remain necessary.

The full prior 185,158-byte cumulative summary is preserved as the exact suffix beneath this new entry. The earlier correction receipt and historical archives are not rewritten. New [execution](2026-11-17_Candidate_Keep720_Service.md) and [receipt](2026-11-17_Candidate_Keep720_Service_Receipts.json) owners hold the scoped source/test evidence; the next preservation cycle must verify its own actual outcomes.

## 🧠 Key Insights

### Evidence authenticity and application usability are separate gates

The raw-report and 97-producer checks make the new composition depend on the evidence it claims to consume, rather than on a portable description of that evidence. They still do not prove a useful normal-service job. The practical path is to preserve this reviewed source, then observe prepare/Start, storage, publication and cancellation using the real runtime in one bounded experiment. Replacing old pins would skip rather than answer those questions.

### Cancellation has an admission boundary before it has a completion result

It is not enough for a cancelled service to reject new work eventually. If cancellation awaits saved state or callbacks, admission must already be closed during that wait. The reproduced race made this temporal contract explicit. That conclusion is supported by the tested synchronous boundary; it is not yet evidence of every real process-drainage case.

### Honest recovery can require a new instance

When active cancellation invalidates the candidate token, that stale-token result is a safety property, not a nuisance to work around. A fresh-service recovery test can show that work resumes safely after full closure, while same-service recovery remains a different and unproven contract. This narrower description is more useful than making a broad retry promise that the composition does not implement.

## 📌 Decisions Made

- **Confirmed technical choice by the lead:** Build a developer-only normal-service composition with exact new runtime/dependencies, original report and immutable calibration producer gates; leave production factories, pins and catalog untouched.
- **Confirmed after independent review:** Close admission synchronously before superclass cancellation can yield; explicitly require cancel → drain → resource close → fresh handle and fresh service for recovery.
- **Confirmed after reproduced cleanup failure:** Preserve both base-service and candidate-close failures in ordered `AggregateError`, retaining the original error object for a single failure.
- **Rejected:** Substitute transformed portable archival JSON for the original raw report, or treat VM-positive construction and inherited legacy failure parity as live media qualification.
- **Deferred pending source preservation and review:** One actual normal-service experiment. The intended next scope is a late two-second range on the 150-second source, a separate eight-second held-out source, collision protection, active encoding cancellation and fresh-instance recovery.
- **Unchanged owner boundaries:** No default/ArchiveCQ29/benchmark changes, HDR expansion, public release or hardware/platform acceptance. Packaging remains last; Ahmed retains acceptance.

## ⚠️ Difficulties / Friction

The historical service harness was tied to older reports and runtime identities that are not available here. Reusing its shape without respecting those gates would be easy but misleading. The new composition therefore reuses validated service behavior while giving its candidate evidence an explicit separate identity.

The cancellation race and double-cleanup failure were subtle because ordinary success paths can pass while lifecycle behavior remains wrong. Tests had to model an awaited preference write and two simultaneous cleanup failures, not merely assert that cancellation returns or that one `close()` was called.

Broad Linux still contains 27 failures and the legacy test still cannot reach a path requiring the missing old report. Those limitations do not block every bounded new source improvement, but they constrain how far confidence can be generalized. No fresh media run was used to conceal or overwrite the missing historical evidence.

## 🔧 Adjustments Made

- Shifted from: Closing cancellation admission only after asynchronous superclass work could run.
  - To: Calling `closeAdmission()` synchronously before that work yields.
  - Because: Independent review and a red regression exposed a pending-write admission window.
  - Consequence: The cancellation boundary is enforced immediately; recovery still needs complete closure and fresh instances.
- Shifted from: A closure sequence in which one cleanup error could mask another.
  - To: Attempting both closures and preserving ordered combined failures, or the original single error.
  - Because: Diagnostic loss would hide which resources failed to close.
  - Consequence: A failed cleanup remains failed with both causes visible instead of being mislabeled or overwritten.
- Shifted from: A calibration-only harness with no new candidate normal-service composition.
  - To: A separately gated normal-service composition and a still-unperformed real service proof.
  - Because: Calibration does not exercise the application publication/cancellation lifecycle.
  - Consequence: The next experiment has a precise consumer-level question without broadening production authority.

## ❌ Mistakes / Risks Noted

### The initial cancellation and closure implementation left real source-level gaps

- What happened: V1 had the synchronous admission window and could mask a cleanup failure.
- Actual impact: Two intended regressions failed in the retained 8/10 red run. No actual new normal-service job, media corruption or user-file impact is established in this work block.
- Potential impact: A concurrent action could be admitted while cancellation was draining, or an operator could lose the first cleanup cause and misjudge the resource state.
- Correction / current status: V2 closes admission before awaiting superclass work and preserves ordered cleanup errors; final worker and independent 35/35 plus lead 97/97 pass on the corrected source.
- Failure Intelligence: The defects and prevention controls are retained in this session and its execution/test receipts. No separate production incident is claimed; actual process behavior remains an experiment to perform.

### Recovery and evidence could be overstated

- What could happen: Idle cancellation could be described as universal external-token revocation, fresh-instance recovery as same-service retry, or a transformed archive as the exact raw qualification input.
- Actual impact: No such promotion is accepted in this checkpoint; the live factory fixes the original report identity and the lifecycle explicitly requires closure and fresh instances.
- Potential impact: A future worker could bypass a stale-token or missing-evidence boundary to make a test pass.
- Correction / current status: Preserve those distinctions in the next launcher, tests and report. Do not silently broaden a failing or unavailable contract.
- Failure Intelligence: This is an unresolved interpretation/integration risk, not an observed deployed incident.

This session created the missing bridge between narrow calibration and a testable real service without pretending that the bridge had already been crossed. The practical result is a smaller and clearer next experiment. Existing media evidence, unresolved failures, production gates and accepted history remain intact rather than being reinterpreted to make the milestone look finished.
