## 🎯 Session Goal

The preceding source work made a legitimate new runtime and a protected candidate calibration route available, but did not yet show that the intended trim behavior actually worked on media. The next goal was deliberately staged: preserve that source, run one short generated-media check, review its evidence, then attempt the longer genuine proportional family only if the short result justified it. This was a learning and qualification step toward useful application behavior, not a shortcut to declaring the workbench or 0.7 finished.

During that continuation, Ahmed said the summaries were not written in the established form and supplied the complete structure. He asked to correct them and keep working. That added a parallel documentation responsibility, not permission to stop at headings, replace the project’s history, or confuse the Blue multi-project archive with FFmpeg’s own single-project record.

The session aimed to:

- Preserve the exact candidate source and identify the actual runtime before real media execution.
- Establish narrow short-case evidence before the 150-second/independent-held-out long campaign.
- Keep measured results, fixed criteria, resource safeguards and publication authority distinguishable.
- Correct all six current cumulative entries to the supplied full form while retaining their original substance and dated truth.
- Leave a concrete next normal-service proof task rather than promoting calibration results into production or release acceptance.

## 🧭 Starting State

The candidate handle, final-plan Keep720 binding and bounded secondary diagnostics had passed their final injected tests and independent review. Broad Linux regression still had 161 passes and 27 failures out of 188. The separate new FFmpeg 9.0.2 build was hash-identified and inventory-checked, but it had not yet run the candidate media campaigns described here. Historical production pins and missing retained reports remained unchanged.

The cumulative session file already contained substantial technical chronology and all thirteen content areas. Its weakness was not an absence of test hashes. It lacked enough of the supplied explicit structure to make provenance, time, before/after position, decision status, actual versus potential impact, confidence limits and exact re-entry responsibilities consistently easy to recover. One entry also used different Outcome/Confidence emojis. The correct repair therefore needed content and attribution checks, not a cosmetic heading count alone.

## 🛠️ Actions Taken

### 1. Complete the candidate source preservation cycle before running media

The reviewed source became local checkpoint `54ee143efed8a05b70520d278dd966ec5b0dda78`. Its [Notion checkpoint 006](https://app.notion.com/p/41bbd7ade63b81cf9411f56f78360542) was independently restored with all 505 selected source files matching and the complete six-commit bundle cloned at the expected HEAD/tree. This preserved source and history, not runtime executables or the vanished workspace’s missing latest bytes.

The corresponding original-ancestry GitHub publication is `11bce729aed4a261c87e6cdd213f29c1f9efe2d0` on `blue/linux-development`, with the expected preceding remote commit as its parent. The eight-path update was read back exactly: 887 remote files, 879 unchanged, four modified, four new and none deleted. Local reconstruction ancestry and remote original ancestry intentionally remain different. That completed cycle belongs to checkpoint 006; it does not automatically include the new media records or this corrected cumulative summary.

### 2. Define an exact-source experiment instead of using an arbitrary runtime override

The campaigns used candidate pair identity `1980b0c0480dba85e00fb69f6994e9fd022b46d6355538ee24a445c87da337b9` and an explicit manifest for its two executables and seven dependencies. The launch gate bound 279 producer files to checkpoint 006, including the relevant process runner and the then-current documentation. Runtime/dependency checks and genuine process-local handles remained in place; a saved JSON shape could not reconstitute live authority.

For the long campaign, review strengthened free-space admission from startup-only to before every child through the existing main-owned execute seam. The seam forwarded unchanged arguments/options to the real runner rather than replacing execution with a fake or bypassing the genuine frozen handle. The reviewed envelope retained one sequential child, a 160-child campaign ceiling, the derived per-case/child limits, a 27 GiB pre-child free-space floor and a 30-minute abort request. These are bounded safeguards, not a hard active-write disk cap or a promise that abort guarantees process exit.

### 3. Run and independently inspect one short actual-media calibration

The short campaign ran from 15:21:00.016 to 15:21:16.309 UTC and exited zero. It used one four-second synthetic 720p30 QuickTime H.264/PCM source, CPU HEVC slow at quality 25 and precision-FLAC output. The three ranges produced 15, 62 and 60 frames with 24,000, 99,552 and 96,000 audio samples. Every selected canonical PCM source/output digest and byte count matched, complete-source evidence covered 120 frames and 192,000 samples, and the census closed all 34 children with maximum concurrency one and zero live children.

The report is `artifacts/flac-candidate-YJ0MBJ/report.json`, SHA-256 `6143eb2f58fbdc047bb490b437a08b69c763dd8bb5f267885a25c62960108684`, relative to this record’s directory. The independent audit rehashed saved source/runtime/dependency files, nine spools and three outputs, checked ordinary-file identity/stat evidence and all 279 producer hashes, and found the narrow result consistent. It did not independently re-decode media or recover every raw streamed byte. Output full-file hashes were established by that audit, not compared with an earlier full-file hash baseline.

This remained the legacy short policy. Its “held-out-open-end” case is another range on the same four-second source, not a separate held-out source and not proof of the proportional long family. Those limits were the reason to proceed to a genuinely different longer campaign, not to expand the meaning of the short pass.

### 4. Run one longer campaign through the genuine proportional selection

After the short result and launcher review, the lead authorized one long Keep720 campaign on unchanged checkpoint-006 producer bytes. It ran from 15:26:30.056 to 15:29:47.313 UTC and exited zero after about 197.3 seconds. A 150-second source exercised a late 40-second interval and an open-ended interval; a separately generated 11-second source exercised a nonaligned held-out interval. No source-generation or output retry is implied beyond the recorded one campaign.

| Case | Selected request | Observed video frames | Observed audio samples |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| Late forty seconds | 100–140 seconds on the 150-second source | 1,200 | 1,920,000 |
| Late open end | 130 seconds to the 150-second source end | 600 | 960,000 |
| Independent nonaligned held-out | 0.413–8.621 seconds on the separate 11-second source | 246 | 393,984 |

All three cases passed their frozen decoded-operation contracts and had matching selected canonical PCM source/output hashes and byte counts. Complete-source checks covered 4,500 frames and 7,200,000 samples for the long source, and 330 frames and 528,000 samples for the held-out source. The campaign closed 42 children with maximum concurrency one, zero live children and empty campaign stderr. These numbers describe actual generated-media execution, not the earlier injected tests.

The report is `artifacts/candidate-keep720-calibration-g1p64G/report.json`, SHA-256 `1feb0f2958c78cc73683c70fed08835d1e61b98462e8b30e0127f95b5cf350ab`. Its status is `passed-pre-catalog-calibration`; `publicationAuthorized` and `catalogActivated` remain false. Preserved source hashes are `b2adc00b3f7e4a2f459023c9dfe484451b8eedfdd3c5114542abc413b3ca893b` for the 150-second source and `0c9512943bb2b0bf542f261b5542389324055de4c77e529e36896e21ed156194` for the independent held-out source.

### 5. Audit saved long evidence without pretending to repeat the experiment

The independent audit timestamped 15:31:54 UTC checked all 279 producer hashes against immutable checkpoint 006 and the still-unchanged working files; both source full hashes/stat records; nine spools; three output identities; the runtime pair plus seven dependency files; frozen bound records; telemetry counts; PCM hashes/bytes/counts; and complete-source counts. It rederived all three resource envelopes exactly. The 21 collector children in the retained case records completed with empty stderr, within the overall 42-child census.

The audit file SHA-256 is `a51874bae8bfb7d4e3d7a345e6538147408a39ce26a388c87201fa4855e3de21`. The lead had separately rechecked the 279-file source gate at 15:30:35 UTC before releasing the summary writer. The subsequent audit still found no document delta. Only after those checks did this documentation correction change the live records; the media claims remain tied to the immutable tested checkpoint, not to a fiction that later documentation has the same hashes.

The audit supports the saved result within its scope. The long audit, like the short audit, first established output and telemetry full-file hashes; matching saved file statistics were available, but an earlier full-file hash baseline was not. It did not independently re-decode media, retain all streamed raw bytes, establish cryptographic preregistration chronology, prove general video-pixel quality, or exercise the normal EncoderService. Frozen chronology rests on the reviewed producer control flow and stored records. Process-local private brands remain private authority, not something a report can replay.

### 6. Reconcile the actual summary method rather than copying another project’s content

The editor read Ahmed’s newly supplied single-project template; the supplied PBS 1.1 skill, full session-writing guide, format-profile rules and relevant lifecycle prompt; the live Notion [Session Summary Working Reference](https://app.notion.com/p/3fabd7ade63b8130a7d2f8092ca52811); and relevant full Blue examples, including the prior documentation correction and FFmpeg continuity block. The six existing entries and their six engineering execution companions were compared against those responsibilities.

The chosen form remains the supplied single-project H1 session / H2 core / H3 chronology profile. The Blue archive’s multi-project hierarchy, unrelated history and historical pauses were not imported as current FFmpeg authority. The repair adds role-specific tables and explicit record basis; known versus unknown time fields with Cairo conversions; goal framing and evolved requirements; decision status/owner; actual versus potential impacts; before/after/current-standing distinctions; precise continuation boundaries; and evidence-specific confidence. Existing chronological actions, rationale, failed attempts, unresolved failures and original pre-publication cutoffs remain intact.

Every original substantive paragraph in the six entries is retained: 236 paragraphs in total. The original checkpoint-006 cumulative file, earlier commits/bundles and historical Notion checkpoint pages remain recoverable and unchanged. The separate initial reconstruction session file is not rewritten. The cumulative current file is deliberately revised, so this is a paragraph-preservation claim, not a claim that all six entire bodies remain byte-identical. The compact [structure-correction receipt](2026-11-17_Session_Structure_Correction.json) records that boundary.

### 7. Use independent review to correct the correction

The reviewer independently checked paragraph retention, exact headings, Cairo conversions and local record links. It identified duplicated provenance rows that made the same lead look like an unknown second contributor; wording that could read as publication before the historical cutoff; an early rejected runtime alternative described as though it had been implemented; collector requirements discovered later in testing presented as original intent; and cleanup described as merely a risk despite five actual leftover fixture directories.

Those findings were corrected. Same-role rows were merged, premature “published” wording received dated visible clarification without erasing the original sentence, the actual cancellation-settlement change became the stated adjustment, later collector requirements were labeled as discovered refinements, and the five directories’ verified removal was stated explicitly. This matters because adding a detailed template can still introduce false history if the editor fills its fields from inference rather than the source.

## 🧠 Key Insights

### A real calibration answers more than a passing harness, but less than a working product

The new evidence establishes that this exact runtime/source configuration can execute the selected narrow short and long generated-media cases under the stated limits. It moves the project beyond injected plumbing and inventory-only build checks. It still does not establish normal service admission, app lifecycle, publication/collision behavior, cancellation recovery, general user footage, resizing, native platforms or release acceptance. Keeping those boundaries visible makes the next task smaller and more useful: prove the real application service, not rerun the same successful calibration to manufacture confidence.

### Source preservation and media evidence have different recovery scopes

Checkpoint 006’s restored source and complete Git history are genuinely off-machine preserved. The raw media, runtime binaries, spools and machine-specific audit files are retained locally and are not silently included in that source backup. Compact records can preserve what was observed and identify original evidence without pretending to replace all of it. A future worker must know both where the evidence was and whether it still exists before relying on it.

### Full structure must expose the causal chain, not bury it beneath receipts

The earlier entries already had serious technical content. Their inconsistent explicit metadata and re-entry fields still made it harder to distinguish who did what, what was known when, and what action remained permitted. The useful repair preserves why a boundary changed and which discovery caused it. Exact headings, hashes and long prose are individually insufficient if they obscure original intention, later correction or the next actual task.

## 📌 Decisions Made

- **Confirmed by Ahmed:** Correct the summaries against the supplied structure and established Blue/PBS depth while continuing development. This is not a request to stop code work, replace the Blue archive, rewrite all historical project documents or change the PBS system itself.
- **Confirmed technical choice by the lead:** Run short calibration first, inspect it, then allow one bounded long campaign with genuine proportional selection, fixed criteria and reviewed runtime/source gates.
- **Confirmed preservation choice:** Keep checkpoint 006 and earlier archives immutable; revise only the live cumulative summary and add a transparent correction receipt. Later facts remain in this newer entry.
- **Rejected:** Treat the short same-source “held-out” range as independent-source long-family qualification, or treat saved-result review as an independent media re-decode.
- **Provisional next engineering direction:** A separately reviewed developer-only normal EncoderService proof using the exact new runtime and reviewed calibration. Its implementation and success are not established by this session.
- **Deferred:** Production factories/pins/catalog activation, broader families, native Windows/GPU/UI proof and packaging/release acceptance. Packaging remains last; Ahmed retains final acceptance.

## ⚠️ Difficulties / Friction

The important engineering distinction was between several kinds of evidence that can look similar in a summary: injected test success, actual calibration success, saved-output audit, normal-service proof and production activation. The campaign reports themselves retain false publication/catalog flags. Compressing them into “trim works” would obscure the remaining application work.

The documentation correction had to happen while an exact-source campaign was running. Editing even a session file too early would have changed one of the 279 gated hashes. Drafts therefore stayed outside the repository until the lead completed the run and released that boundary. This was coordination to preserve evidence, not a reason to pause independent reading and drafting.

Ahmed’s correction also exposed a usability problem in the records: repeating technical completeness did not satisfy the established recovery structure. The response was to restore that structure and its meaningful distinctions, not to defend the earlier entries or add unrelated verbosity. Existing original paragraphs were preserved, so some explanatory overlap remains intentional; the current-state document remains the concise operational entrance.

## 🔧 Adjustments Made

- Shifted from: Candidate build/inventory and injected source tests only.
  - To: Actual short and then long generated-media calibration on exact preserved producer bytes.
  - Because: Source readiness could not answer the real timing, complete-source and PCM contracts.
  - Consequence: Those selected calibration cases now have retained measured evidence, while normal-service proof remains the next separate task.
- Shifted from: Startup-only free-space admission in the proposed long launcher.
  - To: The 27 GiB floor checked before every real child without changing the reviewed argv/options or handle.
  - Because: A long campaign has multiple writes after startup and should not silently assume the initial free-space state persists.
  - Consequence: Admission is repeated across child boundaries, without falsely claiming a hard active-write cap.
- Shifted from: Thirteen technical prose sections with inconsistent explicit provenance and continuation structure.
  - To: The supplied full single-project structure, source-backed fields and transparent dated corrections.
  - Because: Ahmed asked for the established form and a faithful handoff, not just more execution detail.
  - Consequence: All six prior entries remain historically bounded but are easier to resume from; this new session records the actual later results separately.

## ❌ Mistakes / Risks Noted

### Structural completeness was treated too much like memory completeness

- What happened: The prior summaries were substantive but did not consistently follow the supplied detailed structure. One entry also changed the required Outcome/Confidence emoji identity.
- Actual impact: Ahmed had to point out the mismatch and supply the structure again; the missing explicit fields made attribution and re-entry less direct.
- Potential impact: A later developer could confuse an older planned publication with a completed one, infer a model or active duration, or repeat work from a stale next-step paragraph.
- Correction / current status: The six entries were explicitly revised against the template and sources, all 236 original substantive paragraphs retained, and independent review findings corrected. Commit, Notion publication/readback and GitHub results must still be established by the next checkpoint receipt.
- Failure Intelligence: This bounded documentation failure and its prevention controls are retained here and in the correction receipt. No separate incident file or system-wide custom-instruction change is claimed.

### A successful calibration could be promoted beyond its evidence

- What could happen: Saving a passing report could be mistaken for production authority, a normal application job or independent reproducibility.
- Actual impact: None of those promotions occurred in these reports; publication/catalog flags remain false and no normal EncoderService proof was run.
- Potential impact: Factory/pin changes could admit operations whose lifecycle and publication behavior have not yet been demonstrated.
- Correction / current status: Preserve the narrow report identities, audit limitations and reviewed next-service boundary. Do not revive the candidate campaign token after cancellation merely to make a same-service retry test pass.
- Failure Intelligence: This is an explicit unresolved integration risk, not an observed production incident. The next reviewed service design owns its resolution.

## 🧾 Session Outcome

The work moved from a credible candidate implementation to actual measured short and long trim evidence without changing the production trust boundary. ## 🔍 Confidence Level

## 🎯 Session Goal

The code could not honestly progress toward useful trim/resize evidence while its historical runtime and proof were missing. Building an identified candidate and an isolated test route was the way to learn what actually works without laundering new evidence into the old production identity.

The current narrow family is CPU 720p30 precision-FLAC range Encode. Q2j Remux/container seeking and exact MP3-to-MP4 remains a separate unfinished lane. Other audio, multitrack, subtitle/chapter, Quick Copy and final product/native/package work remains separately scoped.

The session aimed to:

- Identify legitimate build inputs and the exact resulting pair, with failed attempts retained.
- Create a narrow, revocable candidate handle and bind the final Keep720 plan to genuine proportional collection.
- Stop after final source review and repeatable tests, before any actual media run or production activation.

## 🛠️ Actions Taken

### 1. Establish the last genuinely backed-up continuation point

Checkpoint 005 is local `9d00ddebcd0ba40d36fa788abf768e6825fab4bf`. Its [Notion checkpoint](https://app.notion.com/p/41bbd7ade63b8120b726c34528de4259) independently restored the source ZIP and cloned the full five-commit bundle with all 501 file hashes matching. The source ZIP SHA-256 is `b20ccf1d0d557da8d4261dfa8dc675de44dee3909625cf73b2de93e4b1ce1690`; the bundle SHA-256 is `522b28b53b8be4374fd5c3282616e0aded62e25331c67d1e5826f4b9aee0b6ee`.

Its reviewed ten-file delta was published on original remote ancestry as `591b6d149396efbca4b89a97792e6cb9c17080c6`, sole parent `39b8d6e528ccb2944e91453de7c2bf2dc561d953`. Fresh branch/parent/tree and all changed file bytes were checked. The complete remote tree had 883 files: 873 unchanged, seven modified, three new and none deleted. That completed publication does not include the new source described below. Local reconstruction ancestry is still deliberately different from the original remote history.

### 2. Acquire and inspect legitimate new runtime inputs

The official FFmpeg 9.0.2 source archive was hash-identified and its detached signature verified against the official release key fingerprint `FCF986EA15E6E293A5644F10B4322F04D67658D8`. The signature was valid; local key trust was not promoted or misrepresented as established personal trust. The source archive SHA-256 is `8c3850283eb25fa026482078a04051e0be17347b09ef81a0849bec15a96e002e`. Inspection also confirmed that this source preserves frame duration in setpts unless strip_fps is requested, rather than inferring behavior from the version label alone.

Five official Debian development packages supplied x264, x265, NUMA, SVT-AV1 and dav1d build prerequisites. Their exact hashes were checked against freshly saved official package metadata. Archive member inspection preceded extraction; independent verification matched all 10,448 regular files and exact symlink targets, with no unsafe traversal, hardlinks or special-file extraction. Package scripts were not run and no machine-global package installation occurred. A transient HTTP 503 was retried at the same official endpoints; it was not a reviewer denial or a bypass through a different destination.

### 3. Build and independently identify a candidate, without activation

The build used a new out-of-tree directory and owned codec/pkg-config metadata. The first configure attempt failed because bare static archive paths appeared before the compiler's test object. The correction changed only owned link metadata to explicit archive selection through the reviewed owned library directory. The original inputs remained unchanged. The first configure's temporary probes were automatically cleaned by configure; subsequent probes were directed into an owned temporary directory. The failed attempt and compiler warnings remain retained rather than erased.

The successful build used four compilation jobs and disabled network support, autodetection, shared FFmpeg libraries, ffplay, documentation, debug and x86 assembly. GPL codec support was enabled. It is not a fully static binary: x264, x265 and NUMA came from explicit static archives; SVT-AV1 and dav1d resolve dynamically to the audited matching installed libraries. No RPATH/RUNPATH, global loader configuration, installation or production runtime pin was introduced. A network-disabled FFmpeg build is not a general execution sandbox.

The new exact pair identity is `1980b0c0480dba85e00fb69f6994e9fd022b46d6355538ee24a445c87da337b9`. FFmpeg is 46,175,848 bytes, SHA-256 `ae5b2e8b48ad0e9b51b89ebab000ebcfc0b76a09b8cb80c88e18fddcc5d9e8ba`; ffprobe is 45,962,728 bytes, SHA-256 `923dab8640da8804c445418b6e2efe4b395665bdb9d4cfc84f6a244ed0fb156b`. Seven bounded version/build-configuration/codec/protocol inventory calls passed, and an independent repeat matched their output hashes. This proves identified build/inventory behavior only. No media calibration, installation, historical report substitution or production activation occurred.

### 4. Add a privately issued main-owned candidate handle

The new `candidate-calibration-runtime.cjs` accepts an explicit inert manifest identifying the exact FFmpeg/ffprobe pair and up to 32 listed dependencies. It validates canonical paths, bounded sizes, hashes and optional soname targets; observes the pair through the existing runtime observer; and checks dependencies before and after owned child calls. Copied public objects cannot become handles because issuance is recorded privately in a WeakMap.

The runner fixes executable selection to that pair, snapshots inert bounded argv and options before awaiting verification, requires bounded child budgets and observes normal child ownership. Common hardware options and non-CPU video encoders refuse. This is a main-owned developer seam, not a general FFmpeg argv sandbox or authority for arbitrary untrusted callers. It adds no CLI, environment, renderer, IPC or production-factory route. Explicit dependency checks verify only the supplied list; they do not discover the complete loader closure or prove general dependency isolation.

Cancellation needed a distinct settlement path. The runtime observer intentionally invalidates a cancelled campaign token, so reusing that token with the already aborted signal mislabeled cancellation as an integrity failure. The correction performs a fresh temporary observation solely to verify settlement, never replacing or reviving the original campaign token. Unchanged bytes preserve the original CANCELLED result; a real post-invocation integrity failure retains both causes. Later calls with the invalidated campaign still refuse.

### 5. Bind candidate Keep720 calibration to the actual final plan

`runCandidateKeep720Campaign` accepts only the genuine verified handle and only the existing Keep720 campaign. The historical entry points, resize/full variants, production pins and catalog remain untouched. Candidate artifacts receive a distinct prefix. A closed or copied handle refuses before campaign directory creation.

For each candidate case, the helper validates an inert source identity, checks fresh source identity and current runtime, derives frozen clock bounds, and selects the final telemetry-enabled plan through `OperationFamilySelector`. The exact issued budget, timing qualification and source-policy references reach the proportional collector. The independently forecast resource envelope must match the issued resources. Runtime, source and selection are rechecked around child work; the case invalidates its selection on closure.

The descriptor expressly identifies its bounds as preregistered clock-derived acceptance limits, not a measured production qualification. The long 150-second source, late 40-second selection, open-ended selection and independent 11-second held-out source remain unchanged. No observed result can silently widen the frozen criteria. Preparation still retains the existing storage floor, child count, sequential census and campaign deadline.

### 6. Preserve compound failures and reconcile final tests

Independent review found that the adapter correctly retained a failed child plus changed dependency, but the long-campaign report serializer dropped `postVerificationFailure`. The fix preserves only shallow own-data strings, with code capped at 128 characters and message at 2,048, without recursively traversing arbitrary diagnostic graphs or evaluating getters for the added diagnostic. A campaign-level test changes an actual listed temporary dependency after an injected failed child and confirms that the saved report retains both errors, balanced census and false authority flags.

Final focused worker tests passed 18/18 and an independent final repeat passed 18/18. The lead aggregate passed 87/87. The fresh all-Linux run passed 161 and failed 27 of 188, with no skips or cancellations. Its same 27 failing names match checkpoint 005's 150/177 broad run; eleven newly added tests account for the larger passing total. The regression is still failed, and failure-name parity cannot qualify masked branches.

The earlier wider candidate regression had 76 passes and 25 failures out of 101. An independent disposable Git archive of exact checkpoint 005 established that all 25 failure blocks match after only repository-path and timing normalization, including names, locations, messages, codes and stacks. They are the inherited Linux execution of Windows absolute-path fixtures in legacy family tests. The common old-test command had 66/91 passing on the parent and 71/96 on the updated tree, excluding the new candidate runtime test; those totals differ because new cases were added to the existing calibration test file. A separate isolated test-only path adapter again passed all 33 legacy family tests. It changed no application source and does not constitute Windows-native testing.

## 🧠 Key Insights

A new binary with the same upstream version is a new evidence identity. Source provenance, signature verification, build success and codec inventory are useful prerequisites, but none recovers old binary hashes or makes old media reports apply to the new pair. Fresh media evidence must join the new runtime, current source, exact plan and retained outputs.

Experimental acquisition also differs from activation. A main-owned preregistered calibration descriptor can legitimately acquire evidence while retaining false publication/catalog flags; it cannot be treated as a measured production family merely because it uses the same selector. Genuine plan binding and frozen independent bounds keep that distinction enforceable.

Failure reporting belongs in the integrity design. If child failure and changed runtime/dependency occur together, losing either diagnostic obscures why evidence is unusable. Cancellation similarly needs settlement verification without restoring authority to the cancelled job.

- Confirmed technical choice, by the lead: keep the candidate seam main-owned and developer-only, with exact runtime/dependency checks and no renderer, IPC, CLI or environment activation.
- Confirmed verification choice, by the lead after review: preserve cancellation and integrity failures together without renewing a cancelled campaign token; retain the preregistered long-case clock limits.
- Deferred: real short/long media execution until source preservation and reviewed campaign prerequisites; production activation requires separate evidence and source review.

The next source checkpoint contains only the four candidate/calibration code and test files plus four new/current PBS records. Runtime artifacts and raw machine-specific records remain local and excluded. Before any real short or long campaign, this source must be committed and its Notion source restored/hash-verified. The planned first real experiment uses the existing short four-second calibration seam, followed only on reviewed evidence by the genuine longer Keep720 campaign.

Production factories, historical source/runtime pins, report hashes, resource coefficients, defaults, ArchiveCQ29, benchmark, HDR scope and accepted releases remain unchanged. A new candidate does not silently upgrade the production dependency. The old 35-file submission remains terminal-unknown and is not retried or rerouted through this new delta. Git → immediate verified Notion → ancestry-preserving permitted GitHub remains the cadence.

## ⚠️ Difficulties / Friction

The missing custom runtime could not be solved by downloading source alone: verified dependencies, exact linkage and a newly identified build were required. Static versus dynamic closure mattered, especially because an owned linker input can still lead to a system-resolved shared library at execution. The report now states that distinction rather than claiming fully static isolation.

The first cancellation test failed before the settlement correction. The final diagnostic review found an observability gap after the initial focused pass. Those are retained development failures, not hidden retries. The wider fixture-path failures also required actual parent comparison rather than assuming they were inherited from their count.

## 🔧 Adjustments Made

- Shifted from: Reusing the cancelled campaign observation context for settlement verification.
  - To: A fresh temporary observation used only to check settlement, without reviving the original token.
  - Because: The cancelled context was intentionally stale and its reuse mislabeled cancellation as integrity failure.
  - Consequence: Unchanged bytes preserve CANCELLED, real post-verification integrity failures retain both causes, and later use of the old campaign still refuses.

Runtime work was separated from production pin changes and kept outside the repository. Candidate calibration gained a branded, current handle instead of an arbitrary path override. Selection moved to the final instrumented plan and passes the original issued collector references rather than recreating policy-shaped objects.

Cancellation settlement gained a temporary observation that cannot renew the campaign token. Campaign reporting gained bounded secondary diagnostics. Verification expanded to exact parent failure-block comparison and a fresh broad Linux rerun while preserving explicit unresolved failures.

## ❌ Mistakes / Risks Noted

- Potential impact: A later reader could miss a genuine integrity failure, mistake cancellation for corruption, or equate a valid source signature with deployed media correctness.
- Correction / current status: Owned link metadata, cancellation settlement and bounded own-data diagnostic serialization were corrected and the final 18/18 focused checks were repeated. Raw earlier failures remain retained.
- Failure Intelligence: The exact failure and control are retained in this session and its [execution owner](2026-11-17_Candidate_Calibration.md); no separate incident entry is claimed. These development corrections do not establish a production incident.

The initial static-link configure ordering was wrong and was corrected only in owned metadata. The first cancellation implementation reused an invalidated/aborted observation context and failed one of 17 focused checks. The first campaign error serializer lost the secondary settlement diagnostic. Each issue was corrected before this source checkpoint; earlier receipts remain available and are not described as passing final-source evidence.

The candidate campaign's injected tests deliberately stop at the first fixture runner. They do not exercise a complete successful media campaign. Pair/listed-dependency hashing is not automatic loader-closure discovery, and CPU argv filtering is not a security sandbox. Source-family positive device/inode assumptions remain a separate existing portability boundary. Stat matching alone is not fresh content identity. A signed source archive does not establish release fitness of a locally configured executable.

## 🎯 Session Goal

The previous work proved that limits reached the service, but successful telemetry still ended at an unsupported collector. The next useful step had to make that real integration possible without turning a result-shaped object, incomplete source read or late revocation into accepted proof.

Connect the already branded proportional FLAC family to evidence collection without allowing callers to borrow a budget or policy by copying its public shape. The next boundary needed to bind the exact plan, source and approved collection inputs, then preserve that authority through awaited filesystem and cleanup operations.

The goal remained one non-resize family and its collector/service integration. It did not include opening production factories, supplying missing reports, changing runtime pins or qualifying long-range media. Existing calibration and legacy collector behavior needed to remain intact.

Initial intention and later requirements discovered during the work:

- Bind collector entry to the genuine selected plan, source and exact issued resource references.
- Later refinement after the truncation red test: require complete-source verification as part of direct collector success.
- Later refinement after finalization review: reject revocation after awaited closure; preserve production activation gates throughout.

## 🛠️ Actions Taken

### 1. Define authority separately from source observation

Review found that a collector policy allowlist alone would not suffice: plan-shaped objects, probes and budgets were separately supplied. A genuine result could still be misapplied if its selected source and plan were not checked together. The private result record was therefore extended with its issued source identity and source-probe fingerprint, alongside epoch and exact plan.

`assertProportionalCollectionSelection` now validates that private binding and requires the same approved frozen budget, timing qualification and source-policy references. It rejects copied results, other plans or source paths, changed probes, substituted policy objects and accessor-bearing inputs. The returned identity describes what was selected; it does not assert that filesystem bytes are currently unchanged.

### 2. Check before encoding and at actual collection entry

The service adds an output-independent binding preflight after its existing source recheck and before encoding. This makes collector compatibility explicit before launching the job, while retaining separate source/runtime freshness checks. It is not a new qualification or factory-activation grant.

The collector accepts the optional genuine selection only for the supported proportional family. It validates that selection even when a caller substitutes a legacy policy. Before creating the evidence directory, it compares actual observed source dev, inode, size, mtime, ctime and birthtime with the privately selected identity. A changed file or initial ctime mismatch therefore cannot pass merely because it is stable during collection.

Legacy calls without a new selection retain their existing admission. Sparse-digest freshness remains outside a stat comparison and continues to depend on the service's source checks. Existing positive dev/inode requirements are not expanded as a side effect of this work.

### 3. Follow authority through the full collection lifecycle

The collector repeats the binding check around awaited directory observation before children and before completion. Source-property validation applies to frames beyond the selected interval. A truncated-source regression demonstrated that successful parsing of a selected range was insufficient for complete-source evidence, so direct collector completion now calls the selector's private whole-source verifier.

That callback invokes a private method, not an overridable public subclass method. The public verification API remains a wrapper for existing callers. The new path therefore reuses the established whole-source contract without permitting a subclass override to bypass its final check.

The positive injected test completes timing parsing and PCM comparison for a one-second source and a half-second selection. A separate 150-second source reaches collector admission, then deliberately stops. The long-source test is not a completed long decode or a measurement campaign.

### 4. Correct the final awaited-close race

Initial independent review found one remaining lifecycle gap. The collector checked its selection after persisting the receipt, but then awaited spool-handle closure. Revocation during those closes could occur after the final check and still let the promise resolve successfully.

An injected close-time revocation test reproduced the issue: 9 passed and 1 failed because rejection was missing. The correction adds a final synchronous binding check after all awaited spool closures, only for completed proportional collection. On failure the rejected call carries the evidence directory, receipt and an explicit historical-not-current-authority warning.

The saved receipt may still describe completed collection work. Its publication and qualification flags remain false, and the rejected call cannot be treated as a current successful grant. This preserves historical evidence rather than rewriting a completed observation to conceal later revocation. The final test checks both the rejection and the retained receipt semantics.

### 5. Reconcile tests with the newly reachable collector

The previous service integration tests deliberately ended at the old unsupported-policy refusal. With genuine proportional admission implemented, those tests now reach an explicitly injected collector-runner stop. Their error is named `SYNTHETIC_COLLECTOR_STOP`; it is no longer described as a production gate.

Final focused tests passed 31/31, relevant evidence/storage/telemetry regression passed 85/85, and the lead's final aggregate passed 123/123. Fresh broad Linux regression remained failed with 150 passes and 27 failures out of 177. A test-only adapted legacy run passed 33/33. Independent review repeated the final collector file: 10/10 passed, with no skips or cancellations.

The final patch SHA-256 is `2ed91323376fd9fdceb23c69cf8e373593bd5327d1a912b5e93a9a3c99f34606`. These final results supersede earlier 30-test evidence for final-source verification, while the earlier attempts remain retained history.

## 🧠 Key Insights

Private branding answers who issued a selection; exact binding answers what it applies to; actual source observation answers whether the selected file still matches. None substitutes for the others. This work joins those boundaries without interpreting a public result shape as authority.

A final check is only final if no relevant awaited operation follows it. The spool-close regression showed that completion logic can remain vulnerable even after correctly checking persistence. A synchronous post-close check closes that specific gap without redesigning generic cleanup.

Completed evidence and current authority also differ. A retained receipt can truthfully describe completed parsing while a later revocation makes the overall call fail. False publication/qualification flags and a clear rejection diagnostic are essential to keep that historical record from becoming an accidental grant.

## 📌 Decisions Made

- Confirmed technical choice, by the lead: require private exact selection/plan/source binding rather than public policy shape or a recreated budget object.
- Confirmed after reproduced red tests: require whole-source verification and a current epoch after the awaited final handle close.
- Retained: ctime-only drift warning semantics and existing production report/source/runtime gates; the new binding does not erase those independent conditions.

The lead kept this milestone to the existing proportional FLAC family, collector/service binding and focused tests. No broader family, runtime, report, pin, default or production factory change was authorized by the implementation.

Initial source ctime must match selection. During-collection ctime-only drift retains the existing explicit warning semantics rather than being relabeled unchanged content. Whole-source verification uses a private callback, and final revocation after handle closure must reject before the collector resolves.

The normal preservation order remains Git, immediate verified Notion source restoration and permitted GitHub publication. This entry records engineering completion only; it does not anticipate the new commit or mirror result.

## ⚠️ Difficulties / Friction

The collector's existing interface was intentionally flexible for legacy and calibration use, while the new family required a stricter genuine selection. Preserving that compatibility without turning a policy string into an authority bypass required a separate optional binding rather than a blanket allowlist expansion.

The initially passing focused suite did not exercise revocation during final close. Independent lifecycle tracing and a deliberately failing test were needed to expose the gap. Service test expectations also needed correction because the collector was now legitimately reachable, not because a safety refusal had been weakened for convenience.

## 🔧 Adjustments Made

- Shifted from: Public-shaped admission and a final currentness check before awaited handle closure.
  - To: Private exact binding plus source completion and post-close revocation checks.
  - Because: Borrowed authority, truncated source and a revocation during close exposed separate holes in a superficially successful collector path.
  - Consequence: Direct collector success now depends on all three contracts, while saved receipts remain historical evidence rather than live authority.

Admission moved from public source-policy shape to a private exact selection/plan/source binding. The service gained pre-encode compatibility validation; the collector gained actual initial identity comparison and repeated asynchronous-boundary checks.

Direct collector completion gained whole-source verification after the truncation case exposed an incomplete success condition. Final closure then gained its own post-await revocation check. Tests were updated to distinguish genuine collector reachability, intentional injected stops and unchanged production factory gates.

## ❌ Mistakes / Risks Noted

- What happened or could happen: A direct collector initially accepted incomplete source completion, and an awaited handle close occurred after the last revocation check.
- Actual impact: Both gaps were reproduced in injected red tests. The unchanged service already had a separate whole-source check; no actual truncated media publication is established.
- Potential impact: An independently invoked collector could return an apparently successful result after incomplete source validation or revoked authority.
- Correction / current status: A private whole-source callback and final post-await epoch check were added; final focused and independent collector repeats passed. The earlier 30/30 review run is not mislabeled final-source evidence.
- Failure Intelligence: The exact failure and control are retained in this session and its [execution owner](2026-11-17_Proportional_Collector_Binding.md); no separate incident entry is claimed. These development corrections do not establish a production incident.

Development also retained a deliberate missing-API red test, a corrected implementation-placement ReferenceError, the direct-collector truncation red case and the service tests whose earlier closed-policy expectations no longer matched the new reachable collector. The direct-collector truncation result was not evidence that the unchanged service could publish truncated proof; the service already ran its separate whole-source check.

The first finalization sequence left an awaited handle-close operation after its last epoch check. This was a real correctness gap, reproduced red and corrected before checkpoint. A saved completed receipt still requires careful interpretation: it is historical evidence with false authority flags, not proof the call returned successfully or a selection remains valid.

A matching stat does not establish fresh sparse content identity. Plain getter rejection does not promise protection from arbitrary in-process Proxies. Neither the positive one-second injected collector case nor 150-second admission establishes real media behavior. The existing source-identity sign restriction also remains a separate platform concern.

## 🎯 Session Goal

A correct formula and wiring inspection did not yet prove that the service would enforce its derived resource limits. This test-only step made the next collector work safer by observing actual service integration while keeping expensive or unsupported media execution out of scope.

Replace the preceding checkpoint's telemetry-wiring-only evidence with a bounded executed test through real service, runtime observation, storage and telemetry components. The central question was whether genuine proportional limits reach and constrain the actual consumers, including values smaller and larger than the old defaults.

The task intentionally did not extend the collector, enable a production family or launch media. A successful test should prove routing and refusal behavior while preserving the distinction between a synthetic contract and measured qualification.

The session aimed to:

- Observe exact derived storage, runner and telemetry limits through real service components with injected children.
- Exercise both accepted limits and first-excess refusals, including the logical stage-size boundary.
- Close the owned fixture namespace reliably without changing application code or claiming complete process/media qualification.

## 🛠️ Actions Taken

### 1. Build an owned synthetic service harness

The new `tests/operation-proportional-service.test.cjs` creates temporary source, output, state and tool fixtures. The source is explicitly non-media; the tool files are non-executable bytes pinned by size and hash. The real OperationRuntimeObserver checks those files, and the real selector derives a source-bound proportional result. EncoderService performs its ordinary preparation and operation-update flow before Start.

Only execution and external observations are injected: a runner creates EventEmitter children and closes them in `finally`; metadata and capability callbacks describe a synthetic 150-second source. Evidence storage uses its real implementation with deterministic available-space observations. Telemetry files are written through the paths supplied by the real service invocation, then consumed by the real parser.

### 2. Exercise both restrictive and expanded telemetry limits

The harness uses 0.5-second and 75-second selections. Their derived per-stream record ceilings are 1280 and 20224, respectively. This deliberately covers a value below and a value above the former 20000-record fallback. In both cases, exactly the derived video count is accepted, while the first excess row is rejected. Audio contains one row; the tests do not pretend to exhaust its independently enforced ceiling.

Successful telemetry collection proceeds to a fake output probe and then reaches the unchanged unsupported-policy collector refusal. The generated telemetry receipt records the expected count and no publication authority. That is evidence of an exercised telemetry stage, not a completed or accepted encode.

### 3. Check refusal boundaries and resource ownership

A sparse file is enlarged to one byte over the derived telemetry file-size ceiling and rejected by the actual bounded-file guard. Separately, injected stdout accounting reaches the aggregate pipe ceiling and an additional stderr byte causes refusal. Retained-storage pressure makes the real evidence store reject the forecast reservation before any encode child. Selector invalidation before Start prevents reservation and child execution.

The harness asserts exact forecast admission, derived deadline routing, expected child counts, final failed status, unchanged source bytes and no published MKV. Fake-child active count returns to zero. No decoded collector invocation is allowed, so a test would fail if that closed path unexpectedly ran.

### 4. Correct fixture errors and establish early cleanup

Initial attempts failed for test-fixture reasons: source size was supplied as a number rather than its required exact string; invalidation omitted its required diagnostic; and a test expected the wrong refusal code. The reported progression was 0/5, 7/8 and 19/20. These were corrected without modifying production code, and retained as separate attempts rather than concealed behind the final pass.

Review then found that cleanup was registered too late to cover early setup failure. Five small temporary fixture directories had been left by those failures. Cleanup registration was moved immediately after directory creation, with conditional closure of initialized resources and exact directory-ownership checks before removal. The five earlier directories were individually verified by exact inventory, fixture bytes, timestamps and absence of symlinks, then removed. This improves fixture ownership without broad deletion or changes to application behavior. If a close operation itself fails, later cleanup can still stop and retain files; the test does not claim an unconditional no-leak guarantee.

### 5. Verify the final test bytes independently

The final test file SHA-256 is `13144c8648ddae8efa86a780cf15dbc7fb44329199daa3cf3c983006db7069f8`. Final targeted execution passed 8/8. The final storage/telemetry/service regression passed 55/55. Independent execution of the final file also passed 8/8, with no skipped or cancelled tests.

The earlier 20/20 core-plus-service and 55/55 regression runs were on the pre-cleanup file and remain correctly attributed to those bytes. No production source change was needed to make the final resource assertions pass. The previous source checkpoint 003 is already preserved through Git, Notion restoration and GitHub; this new test's publication remains a separate step.

## 🧠 Key Insights

A wiring assertion can show that code mentions a derived value, but only executed integration reveals whether the actual service passes that value into its consumers. The two selection sizes are important: a permanently retained legacy default would fail the restrictive boundary or the expanded legitimate control.

Different byte-limit tests prove different things. Sparse logical file size reaches the telemetry file guard; it does not demonstrate a streaming parser's cumulative byte exhaustion. Likewise, EventEmitter closure establishes the injected runner's ownership protocol, not native process termination or operating-system pipe drainage.

Cleanup is part of trustworthy testing. Registering it after setup leaves precisely the most fragile setup failures uncovered. Early ownership registration makes failure evidence easier to trust without overstating what cleanup can guarantee if closure itself throws.

## 📌 Decisions Made

- Confirmed technical choice, by the lead: keep the checkpoint test-only and deliberately stop accepted telemetry cases at unsupported collection.
- Confirmed scope boundary: describe sparse-file refusal as logical size and child closure as simulated; do not claim native process drainage or audio exhaustion.
- Deferred: collector binding and pre-encode compatibility validation as separate source work after this test checkpoint.

Keep this checkpoint test-only and restricted to `tests/operation-proportional-service.test.cjs`. No production source, catalog, runtime, report, pin or default changes are part of it. Preserve synthetic qualification labeling and deliberately terminate successful telemetry cases at the unsupported collector policy.

Treat collector integration and any pre-encode policy check as a separate design and implementation task. Keep existing production report/source/runtime gates closed. Preserve the established Git → immediate verified Notion mirror → permitted GitHub order, without treating the previous completed cycle as backup of these new bytes.

## ⚠️ Difficulties / Friction

The harness needed genuine application contracts even though its media and tools were fake. Exact source-size typing and diagnostic requirements caused useful early failures. Incorrect expected error codes also needed reconciliation with what the real service actually reported.

The main review challenge was scope: real service execution could easily be described as real encoding, while a collector refusal could be mistaken for an encoder launch guard. Both would be incorrect. Evidence wording was kept narrow enough to distinguish actual code-path coverage from absent media and operating-system behavior.

## 🔧 Adjustments Made

- Shifted from: Source inspection and a wiring expression as evidence of resource enforcement.
  - To: Executed real service/storage/telemetry components with synthetic data and injected children.
  - Because: The useful question was whether downstream sinks enforced issued limits, not whether the code contained the right expression.
  - Consequence: The next collector change gained an observable regression harness without relaxing production gates.

Testing moved from source inspection and a wiring expression to executed service/storage/telemetry integration. Controls were chosen on both sides of the old record default, and each accepted video count received a first-excess refusal test.

Cleanup moved from late registration to immediate namespace ownership. The status description was refined to call the sparse case a logical file-size refusal and the child behavior simulated closure. Audio exhaustion, timing expiry and cancellation drainage were left explicitly outside the demonstrated scope.

## ❌ Mistakes / Risks Noted

- What happened or could happen: Early fixture mistakes and late cleanup registration left the harness vulnerable to failing before it owned its cleanup path.
- Actual impact: Early setup failures left five small temporary fixture directories. Their exact inventories and bytes were subsequently verified and the five owned directories were removed; no personal-file change or production defect was established by those fixture failures.
- Potential impact: A failed test could leave its temporary namespace behind, or passing fake-child tests could be overstated as real process/media qualification.
- Correction / current status: Cleanup ownership is registered immediately and initialized resources close before removal of the verified owned directory; final 8/8 and independent 8/8 passed.
- Failure Intelligence: The exact failure and control are retained in this session and its [execution owner](2026-11-17_Proportional_Service_Enforcement.md); no separate incident entry is claimed. These development corrections do not establish a production incident.

Early fixture errors and late cleanup registration were real test-development mistakes. They did not indicate production behavior needed relaxation. The final harness closes initialized resources before attempting removal of its own verified directory.

Risks remain if readers interpret eight passing tests as full media qualification, assume the audio boundary was exhausted, infer real child drainage, or claim the collector prevented encoding. Successful telemetry cases do fake encoding first, then fail at collection. Production factories remain unavailable for separate unchanged reasons.

November 17, 2026, through the final reviewed test sequence beginning 14:16:08 UTC, the legacy-adapted run at 14:16:44 UTC and broad Linux run at 14:16:45 UTC, followed by independent final delta review. These timestamps identify evidence runs, not continuous active effort or an invented session start. Later commit and publication identities belong to the checkpoint receipt.

## 🎯 Session Goal

The surviving project had pieces of long-range support but not the shared selector contract needed to use them honestly. A family-ID allowlist change would have hidden the mismatch. The session instead established who may derive resources, from which complete source and plan, and under what exact clock limits.

Close a useful piece of the reconstructed Linux source gap without pretending to restore missing historical corrections or qualify long-range media operations. The chosen step was one narrow proportional precision-FLAC family: derive resources from a genuine source-bound plan, preserve legacy behavior and carry that authority safely into the service.

The stopping condition was a coherent reviewed source contract with meaningful positive and negative tests. It was not a working long-range UI, a newly activated catalog, regenerated historical proof or broad platform qualification. Scope stayed deliberately smaller than the set of proportional resource formulas present in the surviving source.

The session aimed to:

- Introduce only the non-resize 720p30 precision-FLAC proportional family, preserving legacy bounds.
- Bind resources to genuine current plan/source/runtime/settings state and reject forged or borrowed results.
- Review clock, extent and rounding assumptions and stop before collector or production activation.

## 🛠️ Actions Taken

### 1. Trace the missing integration before changing admission

Inspection showed that the surviving resource-budget module already had deterministic forecast functions, but the family selector still admitted only fixed legacy schemas and budgets. Its common metadata checks imposed 120-second source and 30-second selected-range ceilings before a proportional candidate could be evaluated. The evidence collector also recognized only legacy policies, and the service returned legacy aggregate limits for non-remux operations.

The long-range composition supplied a proportional resource-policy descriptor and a null fixed evidence budget, which the legacy selector could not accept. This was not solved by adding a family ID to an allowlist: metadata scope, source binding, derived resources, currentness, complete-source validation and consumer routing had to agree. Separately, actual production factories required missing retained reports, exact historical observer bytes and their pinned runtime. Those activation gates were left untouched.

### 2. Implement one genuine, source-derived selection contract

The new branch is only `cfr-sdr-range-flac-v2`, paired with `cfr720p30-flac-resources-v1`. It is limited to CPU HEVC slow/quality 25, non-resize 720p30 Keep Source, SDR and the strict precision-FLAC source/request shape. Candidate-specific validation keeps legacy bounds intact rather than replacing them with a globally broader duration allowance.

The selector derives resources from the genuine plan's interval, complete source extent and exact source size. It includes the maximum observed video/audio/container extent, converts that extent upward to microseconds and preserves resource-capacity refusal. Selected duration cannot substitute for complete decoding cost. It binds the source, probe, plan, runtime, settings, input budget/deadline and resulting derived state for later currentness checks.

A module-private WeakMap authenticates the frozen result by issuing epoch and exact plan identity. The public `proportional` flag is descriptive only. Clones, spreads, inherited copies, foreign plans and invalidated epochs do not inherit resource authority. Source freshness still requires `requireCurrent()` at the normal boundaries; the brand is not a substitute for checking a changed file or runtime.

### 3. Route resources without opening the collector or factories

The service's resource function now accepts proportional limits only from a genuine current result for the exact encode plan. Remux and HDR remain separate. Derived values flow to the existing reservation, aggregate byte accounting, child/deadline limits and telemetry configuration. Legacy fallback values remain unchanged for ordinary and legacy operations.

The collector was not extended to accept the new policy, and no calibration receipt, runtime pin, observer-source pin or production factory gate changed. This prevents a source-contract checkpoint from silently becoming an activation claim. However, the collector is reached after encoding: its refusal is not a universal pre-encode interlock. Current production factory gates, rather than that collector test alone, are what keep the actual unavailable long-range composition closed.

### 4. Use failures to refine the tests and core clock boundary

The initial schema-fixture run had 1 pass and 9 failures. The fixture was corrected without a production-code change for those failures. Subsequent tests exercised genuine late, long, open-ended and independent nonaligned ranges; exact resource derivation; false authority; changed source bindings; invalidation; complete-source receipts; and legacy limits.

Review identified a substantive admission gap: the Linux wrapper checked measured clocks, but a direct core-selector caller could otherwise obtain a broader branded family. A direct wrong-clock test was red with 11 passes and 1 failure. The core now requires exact rational video 1/15360 and audio 1/48000 clocks for this proportional family. This restriction belongs in the core contract because resource authority must not depend on callers choosing a particular wrapper.

Review also corrected diagnostics that still described a 120-second bound while enforcing a derived extent. The rounding test now includes 150.0000001 seconds, which must round upward to 150000001 microseconds; 150.000001 alone had not exercised fractional-microsecond rounding. These changes improved the actual boundary and its explanation without loosening tolerances or changing requested media intervals.

### 5. Verify the final source and preserve non-passing evidence

The final immutable patch is SHA-256 `c35704a638a34f822663ee42b599c1cb1463506533c1df44cb546d72611cff0e`, based on local parent `0c5d9d2`. Final focused verification passed 42/42. The test-only adapted legacy run passed 33/33. The broad Linux run remained failed: 150 passes and 27 failures out of 177. These counts describe distinct suites and cannot be summed into a universal pass count.

Unadapted legacy comparison produced 59 passes and 25 failures out of 84 on both parent and changed source, with identical failure locations. Those early Windows-path failures on Linux mask later behavior, so parity was not treated as sufficient. A separate path-only adapter passed 33/33 on both source versions while leaving original test files unchanged. This is stronger portable regression evidence, not native Windows qualification.

Independent final review inspected all changed files and affected consumers, followed forged/borrowed/stale authority attempts and reran the final pure/injected proportional tests: 12/12 passed. No blocking issue remained. Service-to-telemetry enforcement is still supported by wiring inspection and assertions, not by an executed integration test through that sink.

## 🧠 Key Insights

A resource formula is not execution authority. It becomes useful only when a genuine plan, exact source and current selection bind its inputs, and downstream consumers enforce the resulting limits. Private branding stops serialized or copied result shapes from borrowing those limits; it does not eliminate the separate requirement to recheck freshness.

The clock review demonstrated why restrictions that define a measured family belong at its core admission boundary. A wrapper can add constraints, but omitting that wrapper must not produce a broader authoritative result. Exact rational comparison preserves the intended clock value without introducing floating-point tolerance.

A closed collector and closed production factory are different facts. The first limits evidence acceptance after encoding; the second currently blocks actual composition before use. Honest status must preserve that distinction, especially when intermediate source improvements make a previously failing selector test pass.

## 📌 Decisions Made

- Confirmed technical choice, by the lead: support only cfr-sdr-range-flac-v2 with cfr720p30-flac-resources-v1; keep AAC, resize, whole-file and 1440 variants outside this patch.
- Confirmed after review: enforce exact measured rational clocks in the core selector, not just the Linux wrapper.
- Rejected: treat collector refusal after encoding as a universal pre-encode interlock, or promote synthetic selection tests into measured qualification.

The lead chose one non-resize precision-FLAC family as the coherent next application-source step. Proportional AAC, resize, whole-file and 1440 variants remain outside this patch. Fixed resource coefficients, legacy behavior, runtime/pins, defaults and factory activation requirements were preserved.

Review required exact measured clocks in the core rather than relying only on the Linux wrapper. The team also retained mandatory `requireCurrent()` checks, corrected misleading extent diagnostics and strengthened rounding coverage. No synthetic result was promoted into measured qualification, and no missing historical report was regenerated or substituted.

The established checkpoint order remains local Git, immediate verified Notion source mirror, then permitted GitHub publication. The preceding cycle's remote checkpoint `acbc83f` was verified; this new patch's commit and publication are not asserted at the session cutoff.

## ⚠️ Difficulties / Friction

The surviving source contained proportional resource definitions and Linux consumers without the corresponding shared selector integration. That asymmetry made a superficial schema fix tempting but insufficient. The implementation had to preserve old families while introducing distinct new authority and resource paths.

Legacy tests use Windows-style paths that fail early on Linux. Identical failures could conceal a real regression, so the comparison needed explicit qualification and a separate path-only adaptation. The broad Linux suite also retained 27 failures, preventing an all-green claim despite passing targeted tests.

## 🔧 Adjustments Made

- Shifted from: Relying on the Linux wrapper to constrain the family clocks.
  - To: Checking exact rational clocks at the authoritative core selector.
  - Because: A direct core caller could otherwise obtain a broader branded family than the measured contract supported.
  - Consequence: A reproduced wrong-clock admission now refuses regardless of which wrapper the caller chooses.

The implementation moved from wrapper-dependent clock restrictions to a core-enforced measured family. Review shifted rounding coverage from an exact-microsecond example to a genuinely fractional one and separated derived-extent diagnostics from the legacy 120-second text.

The completion claim was narrowed to source-contract readiness. Telemetry routing was retained, but the missing executed sink-enforcement test was recorded as a prerequisite before activation. The collector's refusal was explicitly described as post-encode rather than used to claim that no encode could occur under any independently assembled service.

## ❌ Mistakes / Risks Noted

- What happened or could happen: The initial core branch relied on wrapper clock specificity; an early rounding example did not actually test a fractional microsecond.
- Actual impact: A direct wrong-clock control produced 11 passes and one failure before the fix; the first schema fixture also had nine failures. No production route was activated.
- Potential impact: A caller could acquire valid-looking resources for an unqualified clock family, or the test name could overstate rounding coverage.

- Failure Intelligence: The exact failure and control are retained in this session and its [execution owner](2026-11-17_Proportional_FLAC_Contract.md); no separate incident entry is claimed. These development corrections do not establish a production incident.

The initial schema fixture was wrong and was corrected transparently. The core originally relied on a composition wrapper for clock specificity; the red test and exact core guard closed that gap before checkpointing. The first rounding example did not test the subtle case its description implied; the final example now does.

Remaining risks include assuming a private brand proves current source bytes, treating path-adapted tests as Windows validation, treating an unchanged broad failure count as a clean regression result, or mistaking post-encode collector rejection for a universal launch guard. Descriptor/getter tests do not guarantee freedom from arbitrary in-process Proxy side effects. These limits stay explicit rather than hidden behind passing focused tests.

## 🎯 Session Goal

The reconstructed application rejected the current host’s signed bigint inode representation as though identity were unknown. Correcting that demonstrated portability defect was a smaller, more useful next step than removing identity checks or attempting to activate a route whose runtime/proof closure was still missing.

Move from reconstruction into a useful, bounded application correction. The first target was a demonstrated platform mismatch that rejected the fixed Linux runtime despite valid size, hash and single-link identity. The goal was to admit the observed representation without weakening object identity, allowing arbitrary binaries, changing Windows behavior, or bypassing missing qualification evidence.

The session aimed to:

- Reproduce the Linux identity rejection and make the smallest platform-specific predicate correction.
- Reject malformed non-bigint inputs while retaining valid positive non-Linux behavior and raw stamp comparison.
- Verify the current pinned pair and leave unavailable media/native evidence explicitly unresolved.

## 🛠️ Actions Taken

### 1. Establish a recoverable baseline before further source work

The prior work became local commit `99e1430bf3c2b6156f2388842dc9e0eedb7bab83`, containing 482 reviewed surviving source/build/test/asset files and five new PBS/evidence records. Its source ZIP was uploaded to the authorized Notion project checkpoint, downloaded and freshly restored. All 487 file hashes matched the committed tree. A separately verified one-root Git bundle was carried in a transparent ZIP wrapper after the service rejected the native bundle MIME type; its restored clone matched the same branch, commit and files. That was a format correction, not a review-denial workaround.

The baseline is therefore genuinely recoverable within its declared scope. It still excludes private Planning, executor configuration, unreviewed history and raw evidence, generated media and binaries, the unresolved prior payload and missing latest work. The original remote branch was not replaced or force-pushed. The new reconstruction history remains distinct from the surviving original ancestry.

### 2. Diagnose the remaining runtime identity failure

The broad baseline regression had identified two actual pinned-pair tests failing at the shared observer's ordinary-file guard. Direct bigint stat observations returned inode values `-9223372036854474998` and `-9223372036854474992` for the two fixed tools. Their expected bytes and single-link state were intact. The observer's unconditional nonpositive-inode rejection was the immediate cause.

This evidence supported a narrow representation correction, not a new runtime trust policy. An independent read-only review agreed that Linux signed-64 acceptance can preserve exact identity if the raw stamp is never normalized and all held-handle, path, size, hash and freshness checks remain.

### 3. Add red tests, implement the guard and strengthen currentness checks

Five new matrix tests evaluate the actual private guard in isolated VM platform contexts. They do not add a production API or mutate global platform state. Before the change, two passed and three failed; this red receipt is retained. The failures demonstrated Linux signed-value rejection and malformed-type admission gaps rather than relying only on a new green test after implementation.

The production guard now requires a bigint and permits positive values everywhere, plus bounded nonzero negative signed-64 values only on Linux. Zero, below-minimum negatives and malformed types remain refused. Raw `String(ino)` identity is unchanged: negative values are not converted to an absolute or unsigned alias. The surviving actual-pair test was expanded to reject both an incremented identity and a sign flip during currentness checking.

### 4. Verify final source and preserve the unrelated failures

The final observer/platform run passed all 38 tests with no failures or skips. It includes existing ownership, mutation, hardlink, redirected-path, replacement, cancellation and freshness checks alongside the new matrix. The final broad Linux run passed 149 of 177, with 28 failures. Exactly the two demonstrated inode failures were resolved; missing fixture/runtime/report dependencies, legacy family integration, an outdated static assertion and the independent Remux source-pin gate remain visible.

The final three-file patch was independently reviewed in full. No source pin was changed. In particular, the missing historical observer digest was not replaced with the new file hash merely to make Remux appear available. Compact source-bound receipts and detailed limits are recorded in the execution report and receipt file.

## 🧠 Key Insights

File-identity representation and file trust are different questions. Accepting the exact signed representation can fix a false refusal while preserving equality, hashes and lifetime ownership. Normalizing identities would be the more dangerous shortcut because it could merge distinct raw observations.

The source-pin failure is independent. A focused correct patch is not proof that every missing historical change has been reconstructed, nor that a media route has regained its qualification. Keeping that gate closed allows useful code progress without overstating product readiness.

- Deferred: custom runtime provisioning, fresh media/service proof and actual Windows validation; the owner need not settle routine implementation details to preserve this correction.

Keep the correction Linux-specific and minimal; retain valid positive Windows/non-Linux behavior and explicitly reject malformed non-bigint identities. Preserve raw stamps and all surrounding trust checks. Use isolated tests for platform predicates and actual pinned-pair observations for this host, without confusing either with native Windows qualification.

Keep the baseline records immutable as evidence for their own checkpoint. New current/session/execution records describe the correction and its precise limits.
